171	# Ports from C++ Implementation
172	
173	## Port sphere Gauss-Newton refinement
174	After algebraic least-squares sphere fit, runs iterative Gauss-Newton to minimize vertex distance residuals. Up to 20 iterations, convergence at delta_norm < 1e-14, requires ≥6 vertices.
175	
176	## Port analytic Jacobians for cone LM
177	Replaced finite-difference Jacobian in ConeLMProblem with closed-form partial derivatives for all 6 parameters (apex position, axis tilt, half-angle).
178	
179	## Port analytic Jacobians for torus LM
180	Replaced finite-difference Jacobian in TorusLMProblem with closed-form partial derivatives for all 7 parameters (center, axis tilt, major/minor radii).
181	
182	## Port torus minor-circle acceptance test
183	Added angular coverage validation around the torus minor circle (tube cross-section). Computes φ = atan2(axial, radial-R) for each face centroid and requires the angular span ≥ 0.1 rad. Prevents false positives from strips that don't wrap around the tube.
184	
185	## Port vertex-position PCA for torus axis estimation
186	Replaced normal covariance with vertex-position PCA in fit_torus_sor(). The smallest eigenvector of the position covariance matrix gives the torus axis — works because axial variance ~ r²/2 while perpendicular variance ~ (R² + r²/2)/2. Also added Kasa circle fit for center location.
187	
188	## Delete legacy deduce_*_hypotheses() functions
189	Removed dead code: deduce_planar_hypotheses, deduce_cylindrical_hypotheses, deduce_spherical_hypotheses, deduce_conical_hypotheses, deduce_toroidal_hypotheses. These were superseded by the generic BFS framework.
190	
191	## Interactive viz features
192	Added to \`--viz-stages\` window: initial zoom with 15% margin, help overlay (\`?\`), face number labels (\`N\`) with depth-buffer occlusion testing, hover face info (index/area/normal via ray-cast picking), and click-to-skip-seeding (left-click sends \`SkipToSeed(face)\` to BFS). Integrated egui for all text overlays. Restructured render loop so egui runs after 3D rendering to enable depth-buffer readback.
193	
194	## Viz bug fixes: occlusion, hover highlight, click-to-skip deadlock
195	- Replaced unreliable depth-buffer-based face label occlusion with face-normal culling: labels for back-facing faces (normal pointing away from camera) are hidden.
196	- Added thick bright-yellow wireframe highlight on the hovered face; hovered face's number is drawn larger with a white halo for readability.
197	- Fixed click-to-skip deadlock: click only sends \`SkipToSeed\` when \`waiting_for_input = true\` (BFS is blocked in \`recv\`), and immediately sets \`waiting_for_input = false\` so the render loop calls \`try_recv()\` again and can receive the next overlay — without this the sync channel would deadlock.
198	
199	## Delete legacy per-type trial BFS functions
200	Removed 1,305 lines of dead code left over from the pre-generic BFS implementation: \`run_cone_trial_bfs()\` and \`ConeTrialResult\` from conical.rs, \`run_torus_trial_bfs()\` and \`TorusTrialResult\` from toroidal.rs, \`all_vertices_within_*_tolerance()\` helpers from conical/toroidal/spherical/planar, \`build_vertex_to_faces_map()\` and \`SphericalSurface::vertex_to_faces()\` from spherical.rs, \`vertex_to_plane_distance()\` from planar.rs, and \`viz_custom()\` from mod.rs. Cleaned up all \`#[allow(dead_code)]\` annotations in stage2 and removed unused imports (\`VecDeque\`, \`VizAction\`, \`VizSender\`, \`viz_custom\`, \`viz_face_centroid\`, \`viz_face_normal\`, \`REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER\`, \`build_normal_covariance\`) from conical.rs and toroidal.rs.
201	
202	## Robust cone fitting for pointed cones + prune unselected hypotheses
203	Rewrote cone fitting algorithm to handle both pointed and truncated cones. For pointed cones (all faces share a single apex vertex), uses apex-vertex seeding with fixed-apex LM refinement. For truncated cones, uses multi-strategy axis estimation from normal differences. Added acceptance test with normal-axis variance threshold and half-angle bounds check to prevent false positive cones on spherical/cylindrical patches. Implemented post-selection pruning of unselected hypotheses with index remapping in \`Stage2Output\`.

215	Overhauled cone and cylinder fitting for robustness: set \`REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER\` to 10.0 for aggressive refitting, set \`seed_vtol_multiplier\` to 1.0 (vertices lie precisely on the surface), removed normal-axis consistency check for cones (surface selection resolves overlaps), added vertex recentering around centroid before LM for numerical stability, and increased LM patience to 500 for both cone and cylinder fits. Updated SURFACE_FITTING.md and DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md to reflect changes.

## Curvature-guided seeding (stages 1+2)

Together these enabled the toroidal-before-conical stage reorder. See DEVELOPMENT_PLAN "Curvature-guided seed ordering". ## Detect medium-resolution fillets (stepped_block_toroidal MEDIUM)
All 18 tori + 37 cylinders now selected; the model passes stage 4.1 (dedicated onshape_stepped_block_toroidal_medium_stage41_compare test). Three fixes: (a) the BFS final re-fit keeps the incrementally-maintained fit when a fresh from-scratch LM converges worse — recovered the full-ring R=3.5 pocket-rim fillet torus that refit to 0.87 mm; (b) the cylinder angular-coverage test admits coarse tessellation gaps up to the dihedral limit (medium fillets step ~24° per row, four rows across a 90° tube arc, which the span/3 rule rejected); (c) the default --angular-tolerance was raised from 17.5° to 25° so seed-pair qualification accepts those 24° fillet rows. The coverage allowance is gated twice so it can't admit bogus bands: to cylinders (half_angle≈0), so narrow corner sphere-blend patches can't win selection as 6-face cones (else rounded_cube regresses); and to hypotheses already spanning ≥45°, so thin cylinder bands of a smooth cone stay rejected (else nosecone_truncated over-segments). See LEARNINGS "Medium-resolution tessellation".

13	- **§2.7 design rules (each earned by a measured model failure — don't undo):** (1) 2.7 runs BEFORE absorb — absorb legitimately grows accidental analytic fits with exactly-on-surface neighbors, hiding them from gathering (saddle). (2) Gathering absorbs ≤16-face selections of ANY type across smooth edges (≤1× angular tolerance dihedral; 2× let 45° chamfers glue whole cubes into one component + fit_plane NaN'd on the closed region → "planar to 0.0"); guards: component must contain ≥1 single-face planar, absorbed ≤ singles count. (3) Tangent-plane merge: multi-face plane whose ENTIRE component boundary is smooth joins the region (blister margin = part of reference B-spline face). (4) B-splines are never absorb/refine TARGETS (fit doesn't vouch beyond its region; OCCT surfaces extrapolate — cost arch 1.7% volume). (5) Grid stations AT boundary-vertex pinned positions, subdivided to ~1.5·√(n_verts) interior target: mid-chord stations pin the fit to the chordal prism (arch's mesh is itself 1.7% low on volume!); boundary-count grids undersample dense interiors (8×8 on 946-vert hill_with_hole); vertex-u quantiles produce near-duplicate stations → singular interpolation → f64::MAX garbage.
14	- **OCCT gotchas from §2.7:** \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface\` approximation reports IsDone while overshooting Tol3D by MILLIMETERS near strongly curved patch boundaries (saddle: 1.8mm) — interpolation is trustworthy, approximation is not. \`NextValueOfUV\` Newton settles in nearby local minima below its maxpreci fallback (bogus 1.4mm gaps on on-surface points) and \`ValueOfUV\`'s global search is too coarse on many-knot surfaces — every projection needs a gap re-check (PROJ_GAP_RECHECK=0.02mm, fresh ValueOfUV, keep better), and validation must seed each vertex from its OWN Floater UV (sequential seeding breaks at mesh row wraps). Floater bijectivity needs the solve converged (GS delta <1e-10 enforced; ~1k iters typical) and mean-value squeezes corner triangles into slivers — point location needs a clamp-to-nearest-triangle fallback within 1 bin or hole-fill membranes poison the grid.
15	- Stage 3.3 chain repair now has the B-spline third arm (\`GeomAPI_PointsToBSpline\` through chain vertices, best-of-{keep,circle,bspline} by chain deviation, open chains only) — first demanded by arch's elliptical junction (IntSS degenerate on ONE of two symmetric edges; circle repair cost 1.6% volume). 301	#### 2.3 Deduce spherical hypotheses
302	
303	Fit spherical hypotheses to connected sets of faces that lie on a common sphere. Faces with conical/cylindrical hypotheses are not excluded — a face can legitimately belong to both a cylinder and a sphere (e.g., equator faces). Stage 2.6 resolves overlapping hypotheses later. A spherical hypothesis consists of:
304	- \`center: [f64; 3]\` — Center of the sphere.
305	- \`radius: f64\` — Radius of the sphere (always positive).
306	- \`convex: bool\` — Whether face normals point away from the center (convex, like the outside of a ball) or toward it (concave, like a bowl).
307	- \`error_max: f64\` — Maximum absolute distance from any vertex to the sphere surface.
308	- \`error_abs_sum: f64\` — Sum of absolute vertex-to-surface distances.
309	
310	Runs the generic BFS hypothesis generation with the following parameterization:
311	
312	**Seed type**: The starting face \`fi\` plus all faces that share at least one vertex with \`fi\` (the **1-ring vertex neighborhood**). Only faces that are unassigned for this surface type are included. If fewer than 3 eligible faces remain in the seed, skip this starting face (not enough angular diversity for a reliable sphere fit). Returns a single seed per starting face.
313	
314	A vertex neighborhood on a sphere naturally provides normals spanning 3D, making it a good seed geometry for the 4-DOF sphere fit.
315	
316	**Surface fit**: Algebraic least-squares sphere fitting. Linearizes $|v - c|^2 = r^2$ as $2c_x x + 2c_y y + 2c_z z + k = x^2 + y^2 + z^2$ and solves via 4×4 normal equations with Gaussian elimination. Extracts center $(c_x, c_y, c_z)$ and radius $r = \sqrt{k + |c|^2}$. Optionally refined with Gauss-Newton minimizing vertex-to-sphere distances. Maximum radius bounded by \`bounding_box_diagonal * 1000\` as a numerical guard rail. See [SURFACE_FITTING.md](SURFACE_FITTING.md) for detailed fitting math.
317	
318	**Distance metric**: $| \|p - \text{center}\| - \text{radius} |$.
319	
320	**BFS-specific check — convexity**: Verify the face normal agrees with the hypothesis convexity (dot product of normal with vector from center to face centroid has the expected sign).
321	
322	**Acceptance tests — solid-angle coverage validation**: Computes area-weighted 3×3 covariance matrix of unit direction vectors from sphere center to face centroids. Checks eigenvalue ratio ($\lambda_3 / \lambda_1$) to ensure directions span 3D. Fillet faces along a cylinder produce $\lambda_3 \approx 0$ (1D strip), failing this test. Also requires minimum face count (\`MIN_SPHERE_FACES\`, at least 4 — a sphere needs at least 4 non-coplanar points for a unique fit).
323	
324	#### 2.4 Deduce toroidal hypotheses
325	
326	Fit toroidal hypotheses to connected sets of faces that lie on a common torus. Toroidal surfaces commonly appear as fillets, blends, and pipe elbows. A toroidal hypothesis consists of:
327	- \`center: [f64; 3]\` — Center of the torus (on the axis, in the plane of the major circle).
328	- \`axis_direction: [f64; 3]\` — Unit direction vector along the torus axis.
329	- \`major_radius: f64\` — Distance from center to the tube centerline ($R$).
330	- \`minor_radius: f64\` — Radius of the tube cross-section ($r$).
331	- \`convex: bool\` — Whether the torus patch is on the outer (convex) or inner (concave) surface of the tube.
332	- \`error_max: f64\` — Maximum absolute distance from any vertex to the torus surface.
333	- \`error_abs_sum: f64\` — Sum of absolute vertex-to-surface distances.
334	
335	Runs the generic BFS hypothesis generation with the following parameterization:
336	
337	**Seed type**: Two seed sources:
338	- *1-ring seeds* (same as spherical): the starting face \`fi\` plus the 1-ring vertex neighborhood, for faces not committed to any other hypothesis type.
339	- *Strip-merge seeds*: for faces committed to a cylindrical hypothesis, a 3-ring edge neighborhood — but only when it spans ≥ 2 distinct conical hypotheses. A coarsely tessellated torus (e.g. a pipe elbow) gets tiled by narrow cylinder strips in stage 2.2, each spanning too little of the major arc for a 1-ring torus fit; a 3-ring neighborhood spans several strips and provides the major-arc curvature. A plain cylinder region is a single hypothesis and is skipped cheaply.
340	
341	Faces already assigned to spherical hypotheses are excluded from seeds. Seeds with fewer than \`MIN_TORUS_FACES\` (7) faces or fewer than 9 unique vertices are skipped (torus has 7 DOF, needs sufficient data for a reliable fit). Returns a single seed per starting face.
342	
343	**Surface fit**: Tries **two methods** and keeps whichever achieves the lowest maximum vertex-to-torus distance: (1) **Medial axis / tube center method**: estimates minor radius from normal-line intersections of adjacent face pairs, computes tube centers $k_i = p_i \pm r \cdot n_i$, fits a 3D circle to the tube centers for axis direction and major radius, then LM refinement of 7 parameters $[C_x, C_y, C_z, \alpha, \beta, R, r]$. (2) **SOR axis method**: estimates axis from PCA of vertex positions (smallest eigenvector), center via Kasa circle fit, radii from (h,r) profile circle fit, then same LM refinement. Rejects if minor_radius ≤ 0, major_radius ≤ 0, or minor_radius > major_radius (self-intersecting). See [SURFACE_FITTING.md](SURFACE_FITTING.md) for detailed fitting math.
344	
345	**Distance metric**: $|\sqrt{(\text{radial} - R)^2 + \text{axial}^2} - r|$ where $\text{axial} = (p - C) \cdot \hat{a}$ and $\text{radial} = |(p - C) - \text{axial} \cdot \hat{a}|$.
346	
347	**BFS-specific check — convexity**: Compute the vector from the nearest tube center point to the face centroid. The tube center is $c = C + R \cdot \hat{r}$ where $\hat{r}$ is the radial unit vector from the torus axis to the point. Check that the face normal's dot product with this vector has the expected sign.
348	
349	**Acceptance tests**: (1) Minimum \`MIN_TORUS_FACES\` (7) — torus has 7 DOF. (2) **Angular coverage around the major circle** (same azimuthal algorithm as cylindrical/conical). (3) **Minor circle coverage from face normals**: the tube angle of each face is measured from its *normal* (ψ = atan2(n·â, n·r̂)); the span (largest-gap algorithm) must be ≥ 0.3 rad. Normals — not centroid positions — are the decisive signal: a flat annulus or a cylinder band lies exactly on a one-parameter family of degenerate tori and can span a nonzero *positional* tube arc on a large-tube-radius member of that family, but its normals are all parallel (tube span ≈ 0).
350	
351	\`angular_tol_multiplier\` is 2.0: torus quads twist (doubly curved), so stage 1.3 coplanar fusion often fails on fillet patches, leaving raw triangles whose normals differ by up to ~2× the tessellation angular step.
352	
353	#### 2.5 Select surfaces to use for reconstruction
354	Assign each mesh face to exactly one "selected surface" for reconstruction. Since stages 2.1–2.4 produce hypotheses that may overlap (a face can belong to one planar, one conical/cylindrical, one spherical, and one toroidal hypothesis simultaneously), surface selection must resolve these overlaps.
355	
356	
357	**Greedy area-based selection:**
358	
359	The algorithm selects hypotheses greedily by total mesh face area, largest first. This naturally resolves conflicts: real surface hypotheses cover many faces with large total area, while bogus hypotheses (e.g., a near-degenerate r=139mm sphere from 3 nearly-coplanar faces) cover minimal area and lose their faces to correctly-fitted surfaces.
360	
361	**Algorithm:**
362	1. Compute the geometric area of each mesh face.
363	2. Build a candidate list of all hypotheses (planar with face count > 1, conical/cylindrical, spherical, toroidal). Each candidate tracks its set of member faces and their total area.
364	
365	3. **Greedy loop**: while unassigned faces remain:
366	   a. For each candidate, compute the total area of its still-unassigned faces.
367	   b. Select the candidate with the largest remaining area.
368	   c. Assign all its still-unassigned faces to it as a selected surface.
369	   d. Remove those faces from all other candidates' pools.
370	4. Assign any remaining unassigned faces to their single-face planar hypothesis (fallback for faces on surfaces not yet fitted: ruled, freeform). Stage 2.7 may replace some of these with NURBS surfaces.
371	
372	5. Build a \`Vec<SelectedSurface>\`, one per selected hypothesis, containing:
373	   - \`surface_type: SurfaceType\` — enum: Planar, Cylindrical, Spherical, Conical, Toroidal, BSpline.
374	
375	   - \`hypothesis_index: usize\` — index into the appropriate hypothesis vector in Stage2Output.
376	   - \`faces: Vec<usize>\` — mesh face indices assigned to this surface.
377	   - \`vertices: Vec<usize>\` — mesh vertex indices on this surface (union of face vertices).
378	6. **Absorb redundant surfaces** (\`absorb_redundant_surfaces\`): merge a selected surface S into an adjacent, larger-area surface T when all of S's vertices lie within tolerance of T's fitted geometry AND all of S's face centroids lie within \`--surface-tolerance\` of T. Two situations require this:
379	   - *Spurious sub-fits*: a narrow band of faces on a cone/sphere can fit an "exact" cylinder (e.g. 3 azimuthal strips of a shallow cone have nearly coplanar normals) and get committed before the real surface's BFS reaches those faces. Tolerance: 10× vertex tolerance (exact overlap).
380	   - *Tangent-junction bridge facets*: a mesh facet spanning a tangent junction (e.g. cone–sphere on the nosecone) has vertices on both sides and can't be absorbed by either surface at vertex tolerance; it becomes an orphan single-face planar surface creating fake edges. Tolerance: \`--surface-tolerance\` (chordal sag), only for surfaces with ≤ 2 faces.
381	   The centroid check prevents absorbing flat end caps triangulated as a fan from a boundary vertex (all cap *vertices* lie on the adjacent cone/cylinder's boundary circle, but face interiors do not).
382	7. **Prune unselected hypotheses**: remove any hypothesis that was not selected from the hypothesis vectors in \`Stage2Output\`. Remap \`SelectedSurface\` indices to match the compacted vectors. After this step, \`Stage2Output.conical_hypotheses\` (etc.) contain only selected hypotheses.
383	8. Validate: every face is assigned to exactly one selected surface.
384	9. Print a summary: count of selected surfaces by type, total faces covered.
385	
386	**Why this works better than a fixed type-priority rule:** A fixed priority (e.g., spherical > cylindrical > multi-face planar) fails when a bogus hypothesis of a high-priority type (3-face r=139mm sphere) competes with a correct hypothesis of a lower-priority type (35-face r=2mm cylinder). Area-based greedy selection naturally picks the correct hypothesis because it covers vastly more area.
387	
388	**Nerfed tests:**
389	- (none currently — \`onshape_pipe_elbow_stage26\` now passes \`--compare\`, see note below.)
390	- \`onshape_pipe_elbow_stage26_compare\`: Passes \`--compare\` with 290 cylindrical + 0 toroidal selections. The pipe elbow's torus surface is tessellated into many narrow strips whose normals span < 180°, so stage 2.5 torus fitting doesn't fire (normals look locally cylindrical). This is expected — the torus is decomposed into cylindrical patches that individually pass \`--compare\`.
391	
392	- With the \`--compare\` flag, for each selected surface:
393	    - Compute face centroids and project them onto the selected surface.
394	    - Measure distance from each projected centroid to the nearest surface in the reference STEP file.
395	    - For all hypotheses, report an error if distance exceeds \`--surface-tolerance\`.
396	
397	#### 2.6 Surface refitting (boundary refinement — implemented)
398	
399	After selection and absorption, some border faces may have been absorbed by a hypothesis that fits within tolerance but isn't the best fit; the misassignment shifts the mesh region boundary by a row, which shifts reconstructed edge boundary vertices and corner vertices off the true feature curves.
400	
401	**Algorithm (\`refine_boundary_assignments\`, as implemented):**
402	1. Build the face → selected-surface map; walk every face that has a neighbor in a *different* selected surface (region-boundary faces — the original plan keyed on multi-hypothesis membership, but pruning/absorption destroy that; adjacency is the modern equivalent).
403	2. Compute the face's max vertex distance to its own surface and to each adjacent selected surface.
404	3. Move the face when an adjacent surface fits at least **2× better**. The margin prevents oscillation for true junction faces, whose vertices lie on both surfaces equally.
405	4. Iterate to a fixpoint (≤ 10 rounds; boundaries only move locally), then rebuild the affected hypotheses' vertex lists and drop emptied surfaces. Hypothesis *geometry* is not re-fitted: the fits were validated during BFS; only the region map changes.
406	
407	**Corner vertex refinement** (stage 3, \`refine_corner_vertices\`, runs after tangency detection): SHARP corner vertices are refined by cyclic projection onto their adjacent fitted surfaces — the raw mesh vertex at a corner is only as accurate as the tessellation puts it (where a fillet terminates, the mesh may have no vertex exactly at the CAD corner). Corners adjacent to any TANGENT edge are excluded: tangent surface pairs intersect along a curve rather than an isolated point, and cyclic projection slides along the tangent direction (observed drifting 0.57 mm). A safety guard keeps the original vertex if the refinement moves more than 2× surface tolerance.
408	
409	#### 2.7 NURBS fallback (implemented 2026-07-10, \`src/stage2/bspline.rs\`)
410	
411	For groups of adjacent faces that remain as single-face planar selections after selection (i.e., they fit no analytic surface), fit a B-spline surface. Runs on the raw selection, **before** \`absorb_redundant_surfaces\`: the absorb pass legitimately grows small accidental analytic fits (cylinder ribbons on a freeform surface) with the exactly-on-surface facets around them, which would hide them from region gathering. Absorption and boundary refinement run afterwards, with B-spline surfaces excluded as targets (the fit only vouches for the faces it was built from; a chordal absorb would extend the face into the surface's extrapolation zone).
412	
413	**Algorithm (\`deduce_bspline_surfaces\`), as implemented:**
414	1. **Gather** connected components of single-face planar selections over SMOOTH mesh edges (face-normal step ≤ \`--angular-tolerance\` — a freeform surface varies by at most the tessellation's angular step between neighbors; a 45° chamfer junction is a real feature edge and must split components). The candidate set also includes selections of ≤ 16 faces of any type: freeform surfaces bait the analytic detectors locally (accidentally-coplanar strip pairs at wave inflections, 6-face cylinder ribbons carving up the saddle, 9–12-face cones on a Gaussian bump peak), and left selected they punch holes in the region and leave fake feature edges. Two guards protect real small features: a component with no single-face planar member is never fitted, and when absorbed non-single faces would outnumber the single-face leftovers the component is stripped back to the leftovers.
415	2. **Tangent-plane merge**: a multi-face planar selection whose *entire* shared boundary with a component is smooth is the flat part of the same freeform surface (B-spline local support makes exactly-planar margins — bspline_blister) and is merged in; keeping it separate invents a feature edge the reference doesn't have. Real planar neighbors (walls, bottoms) always meet freeform regions across at least some sharp edges. Analytic models are untouched — they have no ≥ 4-face freeform components.
416	3. Components below 4 faces stay single-face planar. Components planar to fitting exactness (10× vertex tolerance, NaN-guarded: the area-weighted normal of a closed region cancels to zero) become a multi-face planar hypothesis instead — a plane is preferable to a B-spline that overfits noise.
417	4. **Parameterize** the region onto the unit square: outer boundary loop pinned by cumulative chord length, 4 corners chosen where the adjacent *outside* selection changes (arc-length-quartile fallback when the boundary offers ≠ 4 transitions), interior and hole-loop vertices solved with Floater mean-value weights by Gauss–Seidel (convergence enforced — an unconverged map voids Floater's bijectivity guarantee). Quads are split along the v0–v2 diagonal for weighting and sampling. **Closed bands** (a second boundary loop of extent ≥ half the largest — a small hole floats freely, a comparable second loop means the region wraps, e.g. the wall of a closed prism) are first **seam-cut** into a disk along a shortest vertex path between the two loops (\`cut_region_seam\`): the path follows FACE perimeter edges only (a path along a quad's split diagonal would tear that quad across the cut), its vertices are duplicated, right-side triangle fans switch to the clones, and the boundary re-chains into one loop whose two cut copies carry a \`CUT_OUTSIDE\` id — corner detection then lands the 4 corners exactly at the cut endpoints. The clones keep their originals' positions, so the sampled parameter-extreme grid columns carry identical data and the fitted (clamped, non-periodic) surface closes exactly: its two boundary iso-curves share a pole column and coincide.
418	5. **Resample a grid** through the flattened triangulation (uniform-bin point location; numeric misses near corner slivers are clamped to the nearest triangle within one bin — feeding them to the hole membrane extrapolates garbage). Grid stations per axis are the boundary vertices' own pinned positions (stations *between* the vertices of a coarse profile sample chord interiors and pin the fit to the chordal prism — a 1.7% volume deficit on the arch, the mesh's own chordal error), evenly subdivided up to an interior-density target of ~1.5·√(region vertices) (a flat margin tessellates coarsely: hill_with_hole has 8-vertex sides on a 946-vertex region). Genuine hole interiors are filled with a Laplacian membrane so the fitter sees smooth data. The grid is flipped if needed so d1u×d1v matches the mesh's outward normals: B-spline faces are always \`concave = false\`.
419	6. **Fit**: \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface::Interpolate\` (chord-length, C2 cubic) first — the grid is sampled from an exact-on-surface mesh, and OCCT's least-squares approximator reports \`IsDone\` while overshooting its \`Tol3D\` by millimeters near strongly curved patch boundaries. The approximator (tol = surface/8, then surface/2) remains as fallback, and grid density retries at caps 121/181/241.
420	7. **Validate**: every region vertex and face centroid within \`--surface-tolerance\` of the fit, each projection seeded from the vertex's own parameterization value (sequential seeding breaks at mesh row wraps, and neither \`NextValueOfUV\` nor \`ValueOfUV\` recovers reliably on many-knot surfaces). On failure the component stays single-face planar with a warning.
421	8. **Seam-split** (closed bands only, \`split_cut_hypothesis\`): the validated hypothesis is split into two halves along the around-the-band axis (the axis whose parameter extremes are the cut copies), producing two \`SelectedSurface::BSpline\` entries that share the SAME fitted surface object and carry a common \`shared_surface_group\` tag. Each half spans only part of the parameter range, so its two cut edges are geometrically distinct plain edges — no periodic face, no seam edge, no winding loops. A single face would need its two coincident boundary curves to be one seam edge used twice, i.e. the full periodic machinery (below).
422	
423	The \`BSplineHypothesis\` owns the fitted \`Geom_BSplineSurface\` (stage 3 reuses the same object via fresh handles) plus a cached, seed-rechecking \`ShapeAnalysis_Surface\` projector used by absorption, refinement, \`--compare\`, and stage-3 charts/normals/projections. Stage-3 charts of B-spline faces also carry a parameter-space \`home\` anchor (the projection of the region's mean face centroid): a projection landing on a coincident-boundary curve of a seam-split band is ambiguous between the two parameter extremes (Newton, the gap re-check, and \`ValueOfUV\` all cannot tell — both branches are exact), and the branch nearer the face's own interior wins. The stage 3.1 compare **exempts** edges whose two faces share a \`shared_surface_group\` (and the corner vertices such edge pairs meet at): they are reconstruction artifacts flagged at fit time, with no counterpart in a reference model, which represents the band as one periodic face with a seam. The exemption is tag-based, never tolerance-based — the edge compare is precisely the check that catches over-segmentation, and it stays fully armed for every edge the reconstruction did not deliberately manufacture.
424	
425	**Results (2026-07-10)**: all 8 \`tests/nurbs/\` models pass full \`--compare\` (volume within 2.7e-6 … 5.2e-3 relative); \`elliptical_cylinder\` (closed freeform wall) passes via the seam-split reconstruction. Full 477-test suite green.
426	
427	**Path to the non-compromised design (periodic B-spline face with a real seam)**: the seam-split is a deliberate compromise — the output wall is two faces plus two artificial edges where CAD kernels (and the reference) write ONE periodic face with a seam edge. Reaching that form means replacing step 8 with a periodic pipeline, and its acceptance test is precisely *removing the stage 3.1 compare exemption*:
428	- *Fit periodically*: \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface::Interpolate(points, ParType, thePeriodic=true)\` is bound, and the binding docs state the convention: periodic **along U (the first array index), using the first row as the periodic boundary** — the closing column is omitted rather than duplicated. The cut parameterization stays as-is (it supplies the point grid); needs a small standalone experiment to confirm continuity across the closure is C2.
429	- *Normalize the period to 2π*: the entire stage-3.4 wrapped-face machinery (branch unwrap in \`sample_edge_uv\`, winding classification, pcurve shifts, seam-anchor planning) hard-codes 2π as the period. Rescaling the fitted surface's U knots to [0, 2π] is an exact reparameterization (\`Geom_BSplineSurface::SetUKnots\` / \`SetUPeriodic\` are bound) and lets every existing mechanism apply unchanged; threading a general \`u_period\` through that validated code is the expensive alternative.
430	- *Enable the periodic chart*: the B-spline chart reports \`u_periodic() = true\` for these faces; the analytic charts' branch-continuity unwrap then handles the seam-adjacent samples (the \`home\`-anchor branch resolution generalizes it).
431	- *Seam placement is a gauge choice — anchor it at the cut path, don't search*: the fit's parameter origin IS the cut path, so the 0-iso passes through the cut's endpoint vertices by construction, giving a valid seam anchor pair for free. This matters more than it sounds: even elliptical_cylinder's strip mesh has its top and bottom rings azimuthally MISALIGNED (~half a ring step; the cut path is a slanted line [27,10,0]→[26.987,10.329,18]), so "vertex pairs at matching azimuths" do not literally exist and \`plan_seam_anchors\`' compatibility search would hit the edge-splitting error even here. Placing the seam at the parameter origin sidesteps the search entirely; the seam edge itself is a \`u_iso\` through the existing \`make_seam_edge\` path (which already bumps vertex tolerances to cover iso↔vertex gaps).
432	- *Closed junction rescue*: the cap junctions become closed-loop edges again; if \`IntSS\` returns them degenerate, the chain repair needs its periodic arm (\`GeomAPI_Interpolate\` with the periodic flag — bound; the open-chain \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSpline\` arm cannot close a loop).
433	- *Keep the seam-split as fallback* for closed bands the periodic path cannot handle.
434	
435	**Fresh-start notes for the periodic path (2026-07-10, from the seam-split implementation):**
436	
437	- *The around-the-band axis is NOT always U.* Which square axis wraps depends on which sides the corner chooser assigns to the cut copies: for elliptical_cylinder the cut copies landed as the 2-vertex u-sides, so **v** is the around axis (93-vertex ring sides). \`split_cut_hypothesis\` already detects this dynamically (the axis whose parameter extremes carry \`CUT_OUTSIDE\` loop entries); the periodic pipeline needs the same detection, must feed \`Interpolate\` with the around axis as the FIRST array index (transpose when around = v), and must rescale/periodize the matching knot vector (\`SetUKnots\`+\`SetUPeriodic\` vs the V variants). Store the periodic axis on the \`BSplineHypothesis\` — the chart's \`u_periodic()\`/\`v_periodic()\` need it per face.
438	- *Grid data for the periodic fit*: today's cut region duplicates the seam column (clone vertices), which is what makes the CLAMPED fit close; the periodic \`Interpolate\` convention instead wants the closing column OMITTED (first row = periodic boundary). Drop the clone column from the grid when fitting periodically — the clones stay in the parameterization (the cut is still how the disk is flattened).
439	- *Fitted parameter ranges are normalized to [0, 1]* in both directions by the OCCT fitters (observed in \`--stage=3.4 -v\` pcurve dumps); the 2π rescale maps the around axis's knots [0,1] → [0,2π].
440	- *Map of the 2π-hard-coded machinery* (\`grep TWO_PI src/stage3.rs\`): the branch-continuity unwrap in \`sample_edge_uv\` (\`u -= TWO_PI·round((u−prev)/TWO_PI)\`, gated on \`chart.u_periodic()\`), loop branch alignment and the ±2π \`offsets\` in \`align_loop\`/\`BoundaryLoop\`, the wrapped-face pcurve shift, and \`PoleGap\` handling. \`scan_face\` classifies \`WrapKind::{None,U,V}\` from loop windings; wrapped faces take the composite-wire path in \`assemble_face\` (loop + seam + loop + seam), with pcurve convention PC1=FORWARD@high-border. After the knot rescale these apply verbatim once the chart reports the around axis periodic.
441	- *Chart simplifications*: on a genuinely periodic surface, \`ShapeAnalysis_Surface\` returns principal values within the period — the same contract the analytic charts' unwrap already handles — so the seam-split's \`resolve_boundary_branch\`/\`home\` mechanism is subsumed on the periodic axis (keep it for the seam-split fallback, whose clamped surface self-touches without being periodic).
442	- *What each seam-split piece becomes*: keep \`is_closed_band\` + \`cut_region_seam\` (the flattening still needs the cut) and the validation seeding (chord-length params are ∝ arc regardless of stations, so \`arc_fraction\` seeds stay valid — mod the period at the seam). Replace \`split_cut_hypothesis\` with the periodic fit producing ONE hypothesis. Delete \`shared_surface_group\`, \`is_seam_cut_pair\`, and the stage 3.1 exemption — that deletion is the acceptance test, alongside the existing \`nurbs_elliptical_cylinder_stage27_selection\` test flipping to expect 1 B-spline + 2 planar with no group tags.
443	- *Convention probe*: \`tmp/dump_seam.rs\` dumps an OCCT reference cylinder's seam conventions (seam-edge orientation, pcurve sides); extend it to a \`BRepBuilderAPI_MakeFace\` over a periodic B-spline surface to confirm the fitted case matches before wiring stage 3.4.
444	- *Debug probes that will matter*: \`BREPPER_DEBUG_EDGE_UV=1\` prints every chart projection during edge sampling (this is how the seam-split's branch flips were caught); \`--stage=3.4 -v\` prints per-edge pcurve deviations + UV endpoints per face; the \`-vvv\` fit-residual-vs-own-grid probe separates fit error from sampler/projection error.
445	
446	**Implementation notes for the NURBS pass** (knowledge from the stage-3 rework, 2026-07-08; all items below are now implemented as described — kept for reference):
447	
448	- *The chart contract.* A chart provides \`point_uv(p) → (u, v, pole_radius)\` and \`point_at(u, v)\`, plus \`u_periodic()\`/\`v_periodic()\`. For a B-spline chart: \`pole_radius\` is ∞ (like the plane chart — fitted height-field patches have no UV degeneracies), and **both periodic flags must be false**. Every mod-2π unwrap/branch-lift in stage 3.4 is gated on those flags; reducing non-periodic parameter coordinates mod 2π silently corrupts them (this was a real bug on plane charts, masked for months by adaptive densification — see LEARNINGS). With both flags false, the whole seam/winding/degenerate-pole machinery self-disables and B-spline faces take the plain-loop path end to end.
449	- *The chart must wrap the exact \`Handle(Geom_BSplineSurface)\` stored in \`FaceDescriptor.surface\`* — never a reconstruction. (Analytic charts get away with rebuilding the gp_Ax3 only because that construction is deterministic.) Use one \`ShapeAnalysis_Surface\` per face: \`new_handlegeomsurface\` + \`value_of_uv(&mut self, pnt, precision)\` — note \`&mut\` (it caches internally). For sampling along an edge curve, prefer \`next_value_of_uv(prev_uv, pnt, preci, maxpreci)\` — it seeds the search from the previous sample, the B-spline analog of the analytic charts' branch-continuity unwrap. Validate each solution with \`gap()\` (ValueOfUV can return a poor projection rather than failing) and fall back to a fresh \`value_of_uv\` when the gap exceeds tolerance.
450	- *Every \`match\` on \`SelectedSurface\` needs a \`BSpline\` arm.* From crawling all of them: \`create_occt_surface\` and \`surface_faces\` (3.1), \`surface_normal_at_point\` (3.2 tangency detection and the 3.4 orientation compare — use D1 cross product on the handle), \`project_point_to_selected\` (corner-vertex refinement), \`build_face_chart\`, the concave-flag matches in \`assemble_face\`/\`create_occt_faces_all\`, the tangent-edge dispatch in 3.3, and the cosmetic \`stype\` labels + viz overlays.
451	- *Fix the fitted patch's normal orientation at fit time.* The shell orientation of a face comes from a single \`concave\` flag (natural surface normal vs. material). \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface\` follows the orientation of the input point grid, so build the grid such that d1u×d1v matches the mesh's outward normals — then B-spline faces are always \`concave = false\`/FORWARD and tangency-detection normals need no sign logic. Choosing the convention at fit time is one line; recovering it later is a per-face heuristic.
452	- *Edge curves will lean on the boundary chain.* Expect \`GeomAPI_IntSS\` to be flakier against B-spline surfaces than between quadrics, and expect tangent-classified junctions (a fitted patch usually meets its neighbors near-tangentially) to have no analytic construction — both routes end at the mesh-boundary-chain machinery (\`repair_edge_curve_from_boundary\`, \`edge_curve_from_boundary_chain\`), which is surface-pair-agnostic. The B-spline third arm is implemented: the repair now fits line, circle, AND a \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSpline\` curve through the chain vertices (open chains), keeping whichever deviates least from the chain. The first model to demand it was elliptical_arch — one of its two arch∩wall ellipse junctions came back from IntSS degenerate, and the circle repair's 1 mm deviation cost 1.6% of the solid volume; the chain's B-spline curve fits it to 1.8e-2 mm.
453	- *What NURBS will not stress — don't build for it:* seams, winding loops, degenerate pole edges, seam-anchor planning, and edge splitting all apply only to periodic/wrapped faces; non-periodic B-spline patches never take those paths.
454	- *Bindings verified present (2026-07-08):* \`geom_api::PointsToBSplineSurface\` (grid constructor \`new_array2ofpnt_int2_shape_real\`), \`geom_api::PointsToBSpline\` (curve fit), \`shape_analysis::Surface::{new_handlegeomsurface, value_of_uv, next_value_of_uv, gap}\`, \`geom::BSplineSurface\`; the generic \`geom::Surface::{u_iso, v_iso, bounds, d0, d1}\` all work on B-splines.
455	- *Test models: DONE (2026-07-09), in \`tests/nurbs/\` — 8 STL/STEP pairs from \`gen-test-models\` (src/bin/gen_test_models.rs); see tests/nurbs/README.md for the difficulty ladder.* The original sketch (fit reference STEPs with \`PointsToBSplineSurface\` from sampled height-field grids) was rejected: the recognizer itself will use OCCT's fitters, so fitted references are circular (shared biases cancel) and only approximate. Instead every reference is EXACT — explicit-pole \`Geom_BSplineSurface\` lattices, conic/B-spline extrusions (\`Geom_SurfaceOfLinearExtrusion\`), and booleans of those. The doubly-curved patch models ship scripted regular-grid STLs sampled exactly on the reference surface (vertices on-surface to f32 precision); the ruled/extruded models (elliptical_arch, elliptical_cylinder, bspline_wave) deliberately ship as STRIP meshes — full-length strips with NO interior vertices along the ruling, exactly what real CAD exporters emit for extrusions — so the fitter must be designed for 2-ring sampling along a ruling (e.g. a ruled / low-v-degree fit) rather than assuming grid-like point coverage. The conic models' strips come from genuine OCCT tessellation; the wave's are scripted, because BRepMesh internally grid-refines B-spline-based surfaces at any deflection/angle setting (conic extrusions it strips naturally). The \`--compare\` gates are hard errors (volume >1%, round-trip re-read, surface distance), so NURBS reconstruction gets real pass/fail coverage from the first model. Generator-side caveat worth remembering: neither BRepGProp quadrature is trustworthy alone on freeform faces — fixed-order Gauss is ~0.5% low on localized bicubic bumps and ~4e-5 off on B-spline extrusions, while the adaptive variant measured 4% HIGH on a boolean-trimmed extrusion sheet and 0.3% LOW on the same surface as a natural prism face; the generator validates B-rep volumes against the closed STL meshes (scripted ones directly, tessellated ones parsed back) and against machine-exact profile integrals where available.
456	
457	---
458	
459	### Algorithm Reference
460	
461	Surface fitting math details (plane fitting, cylinder fitting, sphere fitting, cone fitting, torus fitting) and algorithmic techniques (Unified SOR Framework, Gaussian Map Classification, Medial Axis / Tube Center Method, Quadric Fitting) are documented in [SURFACE_FITTING.md](SURFACE_FITTING.md).
462	
463	---
464	
465	### Stage 3: Surface Reconstruction
466	
467	Build OCCT B-Rep topology (faces, edges, vertices) from the selected surfaces. This is the most complex stage — surface intersections in 3D are numerically delicate, and small perturbations can cause large changes in intersection curves or cause them to disappear.
468	
469	**Core data structures:**
470	
471	A vector of \`FaceDescriptor\` structs, one per selected surface:
472	- \`selected_surface_idx: usize\` — Index into the selected surfaces from stage 2.6.
473	- \`surface: OwnedPtr<HandleGeomSurface>\` — Infinite OCCT surface, type-erased (created in 3.1).
474	- \`adjacent_faces: Vec<usize>\` — Indices of adjacent FaceDescriptors, ordered topologically around this face's boundary. A face may appear multiple times if it shares multiple edges with this face (e.g., a cylindrical face adjacent to itself via a seam edge).
475	- \`edge_indices: Vec<usize>\` — ReconEdge indices. \`edge_indices[i]\` is the edge between this face and \`adjacent_faces[i]\`.
476	- \`vertex_indices: Vec<usize>\` — BRepVertex indices at corners. \`vertex_indices[i]\` is the vertex between \`edge_indices[i]\` and \`edge_indices[(i+1) % N]\`.
477	- The \`TopoDS_Face\` objects built from these descriptors live in \`Stage3Output.faces\` (parallel to \`face_descriptors\`), populated in 3.4, with \`Stage3Output.concave_faces\` flagging faces whose natural normal points into the material.
478	
479	A vector of \`ReconEdge\` structs:
```

171	    /// seeded from the previous query), plus the projection gap.
172	    /// A seeded solution with a suspicious gap is re-verified with a fresh
173	    /// global search — NextValueOfUV's Newton step can settle in a nearby
174	    /// local minimum without triggering its internal fallback.
175	    fn point_uv_gap(&self, p: &[f64; 3]) -> (f64, f64, f64) {
176	        let pnt = gp::Pnt::new_real3(p[0], p[1], p[2]);
177	        let mut proj = self.projector.borrow_mut();
178	        let mut best = match proj.seed {
179	            Some((su, sv)) => {
180	                let prev = gp::Pnt2d::new_real2(su, sv);
181	                let uv = proj
182	                    .sas
183	                    .next_value_of_uv(&prev, &pnt, PROJ_PRECISION, PROJ_MAX_PRECISION);
184	                (uv.x(), uv.y(), proj.sas.gap())
185	            }
186	            None => {
187	                let uv = proj.sas.value_of_uv(&pnt, PROJ_PRECISION);
188	                (uv.x(), uv.y(), proj.sas.gap())
189	            }
190	        };
191	        if proj.seed.is_some() && best.2 > PROJ_GAP_RECHECK {
192	            let uv = proj.sas.value_of_uv(&pnt, PROJ_PRECISION);
193	            let gap = proj.sas.gap();
194	            if gap < best.2 {
195	                best = (uv.x(), uv.y(), gap);
196	            }
197	        }
198	        proj.seed = Some((best.0, best.1));
199	        best
200	    }

205	        (u, v)
206	    }

1242	    // A closed band (e.g. the wall of a closed prism) cannot be flattened
1243	    // to a disk; cut it along a seam path first. The fitted surface is then
1244	    // split into two faces (see split_cut_hypothesis) — its two parameter-
1245	    // extreme boundary curves coincide, and a single face would need the
1246	    // full periodic-seam machinery (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN §2.7 follow-up).
1247	    let cut = is_closed_band(&region);
1248	    let region = if cut {
1249	        cut_region_seam(region, mesh, config.verbosity)?
1250	    } else {
1251	        region
1252	    };
1253	    let outer = outer_loop_index(&region);
1254	    if config.verbosity >= 2 {
1255	        let loop_lens: Vec<usize> = region.loops.iter().map(|l| l.len()).collect();
1256	        eprintln!(
1257	            "  Stage 2.7: region {} faces, {} verts, {} tris, boundary loops {:?} (outer = #{outer}{})",
1258	            region.faces.len(),
1259	            region.verts.len(),
1260	            region.tris.len(),
1261	            loop_lens,
1262	            if cut { ", seam-cut" } else { "" },
1263	        );
1264	    }
1265	    if config.verbosity >= 3 {
1266	        let pts: Vec<[f64; 3]> = region.loops[outer]
1267	            .iter()
1268	            .step_by((region.loops[outer].len() / 24).max(1))
1269	            .map(|&(li, _)| region.positions[li])
1270	            .collect();
1271	        eprintln!("  Stage 2.7: outer loop samples: {pts:.3?}");
1272	    }
1273	
1274	    // Parameterize: outer loop pinned to the unit square, everything else
1275	    // (interior and hole-boundary vertices) solved with mean-value weights.
1276	    let nv = region.verts.len();
1277	    let mut uv = vec![(0.0_f64, 0.0_f64); nv];
1278	    let mut pinned = vec![false; nv];
1279	    let lp = &region.loops[outer];
1280	    let corners = choose_corners(lp, &region.positions);
1281	    if corners.len() != 4 {
1282	        return Err(format!(
1283	            "could not pin 4 boundary corners (found {})",
1284	            corners.len()
1285	        ));
1286	    }
1287	    map_outer_loop_to_square(lp, &corners, &region.positions, &mut uv);
1288	    for &(li, _) in lp {
1289	        pinned[li] = true;
1290	    }
1291	    solve_interior_uv(&region, &pinned, &mut uv, config.verbosity)?;
1292	
1293	    // Grid resolution baseline: the mesh's own boundary density (sides of
1294	    // the square). Oversampled to avoid aliasing between vertices; the
1295	    // retry ladder below raises density when validation fails (the Floater
1296	    // map compresses high-curvature areas, so a uniform UV grid can
1297	    // undersample them in 3D).
1298	    let n = lp.len();
1299	    let side_len = |s: usize| (corners[(s + 1) % 4] + n - corners[s]) % n + 1;
1300	    let base_u = side_len(0).max(side_len(2));
1301	    let base_v = side_len(1).max(side_len(3));
1302	
1303	    if config.verbosity >= 2 {
1304	        let corner_pts: Vec<[f64; 3]> = corners
1305	            .iter()
1306	            .map(|&c| region.positions[lp[c].0])
1307	            .collect();
1308	        eprintln!(
1309	            "  Stage 2.7: sides {}x{} verts, corners at {:?}",
1310	            base_u, base_v, corner_pts,
1311	        );
1312	    }
1313	
1314	    // Pinned positions of the boundary vertices along each pair of opposite
1315	    // sides: the base grid stations (see \`axis_stations\`).
1316	    let side_coords = |s: usize, take_u: bool| -> Vec<f64> {
1317	        let n = lp.len();
1318	        let len = (corners[(s + 1) % 4] + n - corners[s]) % n;
1319	        let mut out: Vec<f64> = (0..=len)
1320	            .map(|e| {
1321	                let li = lp[(corners[s] + e) % n].0;
1322	                if take_u { uv[li].0 } else { uv[li].1 }
1323	            })
1324	            .collect();
1325	        out.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap());
1326	        out
1327	    };
1328	    let (u_side_a, u_side_b) = (side_coords(0, true), side_coords(2, true));
1329	    let (v_side_a, v_side_b) = (side_coords(1, false), side_coords(3, false));
1330	
1331	    let sampler = UvSampler::new(&region, &uv);
1332	    let maps = AxisMaps::build(&sampler);
1333	    let mut last_err = String::new();
1334	    for (density, cap) in [(1.5, 121), (2.5, 181), (3.5, 241)] {
1335	        // Interior data density sets the enrichment target: an axis's
1336	        // boundary can be much coarser than the region it bounds (flat
1337	        // margins tessellate coarsely — an 8-vertex side on a 946-vertex
1338	        // region).
1339	        let target = ((region.verts.len() as f64).sqrt() * density).round() as usize;
1340	        let target = target.clamp(8, cap);
1341	        let u_stations = axis_stations(&u_side_a, &u_side_b, target, &maps.u_profile);
1342	        let v_stations = axis_stations(&v_side_a, &v_side_b, target, &maps.v_profile);
1343	        match fit_grid(mesh, &region, &sampler, &maps, &u_stations, &v_stations, config) {
1344	            Ok(hyp) => {
1345	                return if cut {
1346	                    split_cut_hypothesis(hyp, mesh, &region, &uv)
1347	                } else {
1348	                    Ok(vec![hyp])
1349	                };
1350	            }
1351	            Err(e) => {
1352	                if config.verbosity >= 2 {
1353	                    eprintln!(
1354	                        "  Stage 2.7: {}x{} grid attempt failed: {e}",
1355	                        u_stations.len(),
1356	                        v_stations.len(),
1357	                    );
1358	                }
1359	                // A collapsed parameterization won't improve with density.
1360	                if e.contains("parameterization covers") {
1361	                    return Err(e);
1362	                }
1363	                last_err = e;
1364	            }
1365	        }
1366	    }
1367	    Err(last_err)
1368	}
1369	
1370	/// Split the fitted hypothesis of a seam-cut closed band into two halves
1371	/// along the around-the-band axis. Both halves share the SAME fitted
1372	/// surface object; the caller tags them with a common
1373	/// \`shared_surface_group\` so downstream code can recognize the two edges
1374	/// between them as reconstruction artifacts (they have no counterpart in a
1375	/// reference model, which would use one periodic face with a seam). Each
1376	/// half spans only part of the surface's parameter range, so its two cut
1377	/// edges are geometrically distinct and no periodic-seam machinery is
1378	/// needed.
1379	fn split_cut_hypothesis(
1380	    hyp: BSplineHypothesis,
1381	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
1382	    region: &Region,
1383	    uv: &[(f64, f64)],
1384	) -> Result<Vec<BSplineHypothesis>, String> {
1385	    // The around axis runs from cut copy to cut copy: cut vertices sit at
1386	    // that axis's parameter extremes.
1387	    let cut_uv = region
1388	        .loops
1389	        .iter()
1390	        .flatten()
1391	        .find(|&&(_, out)| out == CUT_OUTSIDE)
1392	        .map(|&(li, _)| uv[li])
1393	        .ok_or("seam-cut region has no cut boundary")?;
1394	    let around_u = cut_uv.0.min(1.0 - cut_uv.0) < cut_uv.1.min(1.0 - cut_uv.1);
1395	
1396	    // Mean around-coordinate per region face, from its triangles.
1397	    let mut face_sum = vec![(0.0_f64, 0usize); region.faces.len()];
1398	    for (ti, tri) in region.tris.iter().enumerate() {
1399	        let rf = region.tri_face[ti];
1400	        for &li in tri {
1401	            face_sum[rf].0 += if around_u { uv[li].0 } else { uv[li].1 };
1402	            face_sum[rf].1 += 1;
1403	        }
1404	    }
1405	    let mut faces_a = Vec::new();
1406	    let mut faces_b = Vec::new();
1407	    for (rf, &fi) in region.faces.iter().enumerate() {
1408	        let mean = face_sum[rf].0 / face_sum[rf].1.max(1) as f64;
1409	        if std::env::var_os("BREPPER_DEBUG_SPLIT").is_some() && !(0.05..=0.95).contains(&mean) {
1410	            let corners: Vec<(usize, f64, f64)> = region
1411	                .tris
1412	                .iter()
1413	                .enumerate()
1414	                .filter(|(ti, _)| region.tri_face[*ti] == rf)
1415	                .flat_map(|(_, tri)| tri.iter().map(|&li| (li, uv[li].0, uv[li].1)))
1416	                .collect();
1417	            eprintln!("      split face {fi}: mean={mean:.4} corners={corners:.4?}");
1418	        }
1419	        if mean < 0.5 {
1420	            faces_a.push(fi);
1421	        } else {
1422	            faces_b.push(fi);
1423	        }
1424	    }
1425	    if faces_a.is_empty() || faces_b.is_empty() {
1426	        return Err("seam split left one half empty".to_string());
1427	    }
1428	    let verts_of = |faces: &[usize]| -> Vec<usize> {
1429	        let mut vs: Vec<usize> = faces
1430	            .iter()
1431	            .flat_map(|&fi| {
1432	                let f = &mesh.faces[fi];
1433	                f.vertex_indices[..f.vertex_count as usize].to_vec()
1434	            })
1435	            .collect();
1436	        vs.sort_unstable();
1437	        vs.dedup();
1438	        vs
1439	    };
1440	
1441	    let surface_b = hyp
1442	        .surface
1443	        .to_handle_surface()
1444	        .downcast_to_b_spline_surface()
1445	        .ok_or("could not mint a second handle to the fitted surface")?;
1446	    let mut hyp_b = BSplineHypothesis::new(surface_b, faces_b.clone(), verts_of(&faces_b));
1447	    hyp_b.error_max = hyp.error_max;
1448	    hyp_b.centroid_error_max = hyp.centroid_error_max;
1449	    hyp_b.error_abs_sum = hyp.error_abs_sum;
1450	
1451	    let mut hyp_a = hyp;
1452	    hyp_a.vertices = verts_of(&faces_a);
1453	    hyp_a.faces = faces_a;
1454	
1455	    Ok(vec![hyp_a, hyp_b])
1456	}
1457	
1458	/// Grid stations for one axis. The base stations are the boundary
1459	/// vertices' own pinned positions (averaged between the two opposite
1460	/// sides — structured or not, the boundary is where the region has exact
1461	/// on-surface data at exact parameters): stations *between* the vertices
1462	/// of a coarse profile sample chord interiors and pin the interpolant to
1463	/// the chordal prism (a 1.7% volume deficit on the arch, the mesh's own
1464	/// chordal error). The base is then evenly subdivided up to the interior-
1465	/// density target when the boundary is coarser than the region it bounds
1466	/// (flat margins tessellate coarsely: hill_with_hole has 8-vertex sides on
1467	/// a 946-vertex region, and an 8×8 grid cannot carry the bump). Sides with
1468	/// mismatched vertex counts fall back to arc-length-uniform stations.
1469	fn axis_stations(
1470	    side_a: &[f64],
1471	    side_b: &[f64],
1472	    target: usize,
1473	    profile: &[(f64, f64)],
1474	) -> Vec<f64> {
1475	    if side_a.len() != side_b.len() || side_a.len() < 2 {
1476	        return AxisMaps::stations(profile, target);
1477	    }
1478	    let base: Vec<f64> = side_a
1479	        .iter()
1480	        .zip(side_b)
1481	        .map(|(a, b)| ((a + b) / 2.0).clamp(0.0, 1.0))
1482	        .collect();
1483	    let k = (target.saturating_sub(1)).div_ceil(base.len() - 1).max(1);
1484	    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity((base.len() - 1) * k + 1);
1485	    for w in base.windows(2) {
1486	        for step in 0..k {
1487	            out.push(w[0] + (w[1] - w[0]) * step as f64 / k as f64);
1488	        }
1489	    }
```
</details>

1624	/// Validate a fitted surface: every region vertex and face centroid must
1625	/// lie within surface tolerance. Each projection is seeded from the
1626	/// vertex's own Floater UV (\`uv\`, indexed like \`region.verts\`), the one
1627	/// reliable way to find the true nearest point on a many-knot surface.
1628	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
1629	fn validate_fit(
1630	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
1631	    region: &Region,
1632	    uv: &[(f64, f64)],
1633	    maps: &AxisMaps,
1634	    flip: bool,
1635	    surface: OwnedPtr<geom::HandleGeomBSplineSurface>,
1636	    n_u: usize,
1637	    n_v: usize,
1638	    grid: &[[f64; 3]],
1639	    config: &Config,
1640	) -> Result<BSplineHypothesis, String> {
1641	    // Hypothesis vertices are unique GLOBAL mesh vertices (region.verts may
1642	    // repeat globals for seam-cut clones).
1643	    let mut hyp_verts = region.verts.clone();
1644	    hyp_verts.sort_unstable();
1645	    hyp_verts.dedup();
1646	    let mut hyp = BSplineHypothesis::new(surface, region.faces.clone(), hyp_verts);
1647	    let tol = config.surface_tolerance_mm;
1648	    // The fit's parameter is ~linear in arc fraction (the grid stations
1649	    // equalize arc), and its v axis is reversed when the grid was flipped
1650	    // for outward orientation.
1651	    let seed_of = |li: usize| -> (f64, f64) {
1652	        let (u, v) = uv[li];
1653	        let gu = AxisMaps::arc_fraction(&maps.u_profile, u);
1654	        let gv = AxisMaps::arc_fraction(&maps.v_profile, v);

1661	        // validation projection instead.
1662	        let mut max_gap = 0.0_f64;
1663	        let mut max_at = [0.0_f64; 3];
1664	        for p in grid.iter().step_by(3) {
1665	            let d = hyp.distance(p);
1666	            if d > max_gap {
1667	                max_gap = d;
1668	                max_at = *p;
1669	            }

4211	            ChartKind::Torus { major_radius, minor_radius } => {
4212	                (major_radius + minor_radius * v.cos(), minor_radius * v.sin())

4217	            self.origin[0] + rad * (cu * self.x[0] + su * self.y[0]) + h * self.z[0],

4223	    /// The V parameter and 3D position of this chart's pole on the given
4224	    /// side (sign of V), if the surface has one there. Cones have a single
4225	    /// pole (the apex, V=0); spheres have poles at V=±π/2.
4226	    fn pole(&self, positive_v_side: bool) -> Option<(f64, [f64; 3])> {
4227	        match self.kind {
4228	            ChartKind::Cone { .. } => {
4229	                // The apex (V=0) bounds the face from below only: our cone
4230	                // axes point from apex toward the mesh, so faces live at V>0.
4231	                if positive_v_side {
4232	                    None
4233	                } else {
4234	                    Some((0.0, self.origin))
4235	                }
4236	            }
4237	            ChartKind::Sphere { radius } => {
4238	                let sign = if positive_v_side { 1.0 } else { -1.0 };
4239	                let v = sign * std::f64::consts::FRAC_PI_2;
4240	                Some((
4241	                    v,
4242	                    [
4243	                        self.origin[0] + sign * radius * self.z[0],
4244	                        self.origin[1] + sign * radius * self.z[1],
4245	                        self.origin[2] + sign * radius * self.z[2],
4246	                    ],
4247	                ))
4248	            }
4249	            _ => None,

4254	/// Build the analytic chart for a face, together with a replacement OCCT
4255	/// surface when the chart requires one (spheres are re-axised).
4256	///
4257	/// The sphere axis is a pure gauge choice — nothing in the B-rep depends on
4258	/// it — so we point it at the mesh region's mean direction from the center.
4259	/// This guarantees the region's boundary stays away from the UV poles: the
4260	/// poles land either strictly inside the region (a polar cap, handled by a
4261	/// degenerate pole edge) or strictly outside it. That one choice replaces
4262	/// all of the old pole-proximity special cases.
4263	fn build_face_chart(
4264	    fd: &FaceDescriptor,
4265	    stage2: &Stage2Output,
4266	) -> (SurfaceChart, Option<OwnedPtr<geom::HandleGeomSurface>>) {
4267	    let surface = &stage2.selected_surfaces[fd.selected_surface_idx];
4268	    let ax3_frame = |origin: [f64; 3], dir: [f64; 3]| -> ([f64; 3], [f64; 3], [f64; 3]) {
4269	        let o = gp::Pnt::new_real3(origin[0], origin[1], origin[2]);
4270	        let d = gp::Dir::new_real3(dir[0], dir[1], dir[2]);
4271	        let ax3 = gp::Ax3::new_pnt_dir(&o, &d);
4272	        let x = ax3.x_direction();
4273	        let y = ax3.y_direction();
4274	        let z = ax3.direction();
4275	        (
4276	            [x.x(), x.y(), x.z()],
4277	            [y.x(), y.y(), y.z()],
4278	            [z.x(), z.y(), z.z()],
4279	        )
4280	    };
4281	    match surface {
4282	        SelectedSurface::Planar(idx) => {
4283	            let hyp = &stage2.planar_hypotheses[*idx];
4284	            let origin = [
4285	                hyp.normal[0] * hyp.distance,
4286	                hyp.normal[1] * hyp.distance,
4287	                hyp.normal[2] * hyp.distance,
4288	            ];
4289	            let (x, y, z) = ax3_frame(origin, hyp.normal);
4290	            (SurfaceChart { origin, x, y, z, kind: ChartKind::Plane, bspline: None }, None)
4291	        }
4292	        SelectedSurface::Conical(idx) => {
4293	            let hyp = &stage2.conical_hypotheses[*idx];
4294	            let (x, y, z) = ax3_frame(hyp.apex, hyp.axis_direction);
4295	            let kind = if hyp.is_cylinder() {
4296	                ChartKind::Cylinder { radius: hyp.radius_at_apex }
4297	            } else {
4298	                ChartKind::Cone { half_angle: hyp.half_angle }
4299	            };
4300	            (SurfaceChart { origin: hyp.apex, x, y, z, kind, bspline: None }, None)
4301	        }
