4629	            }
4630	        }
4631	    }
4632	
4633	    // Winding: net change over the full cycle, including the closing junction.
4634	    let (first_start, _, first_start_pole, _) = ends(&items[0], offsets[0]);
4635	    let (_, last_end, _, last_end_pole) = ends(&items[n - 1], offsets[n - 1]);
4636	    let mut wu = 0i32;
4637	    let mut wv = 0i32;
4638	    if chart.u_periodic() {
4639	        if last_end_pole && first_start_pole {
4640	            if (last_end.0 - first_start.0).abs() > 1e-6 {
4641	                pole_gaps.push(PoleGap {
4642	                    after_item: n - 1,
4643	                    u_from: last_end.0,
4644	                    u_to: first_start.0,
4645	                    positive_v_side: last_end.1 > 0.0,
4646	                });
4647	            }
4648	        } else {
4649	            wu = ((last_end.0 - first_start.0) / TWO_PI).round() as i32;
4650	        }
4651	    }
4652	    if chart.v_periodic() {
4653	        wv = ((last_end.1 - first_start.1) / TWO_PI).round() as i32;
4654	    }
4655	    Ok(BoundaryLoop { items, offsets, winding_u: wu, winding_v: wv, pole_gaps })
4656	}
4657	
4658	/// Which periodic direction (if any) a face's boundary wraps all the way
4659	/// around, requiring a seam edge.
4660	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
4661	enum WrapKind {
4662	    None,
4663	    U,
4664	    V,
4665	}
4666	
4667	/// How the seam endpoint on a winding boundary loop is realized.
4668	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
4669	enum AnchorSource {
4670	    /// An existing shared BRepVertex (index into Stage3Output.vertices).
4671	    Vertex(usize),
4672	    /// A closed-loop edge re-ranged to start at the seam; the anchor vertex
4673	    /// lives in AssemblyCtx.closed_anchors under this edge id.
4674	    ClosedEdge(usize),
4675	}
4676	
4677	/// Per-face seam plan: one anchor per winding boundary loop, in the face's
4678	/// deterministic loop-scan order.
4679	struct WrapPlan {
4680	    anchors: Vec<AnchorSource>,
4681	}
4682	
4683	/// Shared assembly state for stage 3.4.
4684	struct AssemblyCtx {
4685	    topo_vertices: Vec<OwnedPtr<topo_ds::Vertex>>,
4686	    topo_edges: Vec<OwnedPtr<topo_ds::Edge>>,
4687	    /// BRepVertex ids [start, end] of each edge in stored-curve direction
4688	    /// (both usize::MAX for closed-loop edges).
4689	    edge_vertices: Vec<[usize; 2]>,
4690	    /// Seam-anchor vertices added to re-ranged closed-loop edges.
4691	    closed_anchors: HashMap<usize, OwnedPtr<topo_ds::Vertex>>,
4692	}
4693	
4694	/// Pole-detection radius: below this distance from the chart's degeneracy
4695	/// locus, a sample's U coordinate is treated as meaningless.
4696	fn pole_radius_eps(config: &Config) -> f64 {
4697	    (10.0 * config.vertex_tolerance_mm).max(1e-6)
4698	}
4699	
4700	/// Number of samples per edge for UV analysis and pcurve interpolation.
4701	const PCURVE_BASE_SAMPLES: usize = 33;
4702	
4703	/// Scan of one face's boundary in UV: chart, per-edge samples, aligned
4704	/// loops, and the wrap classification.
4705	struct FaceScan {
4706	    chart: SurfaceChart,
4707	    surface_override: Option<OwnedPtr<geom::HandleGeomSurface>>,
4708	    samples: HashMap<usize, EdgeUv>,
4709	    loops: Vec<BoundaryLoop>,
4710	    wrap: WrapKind,
4711	}
4712	
4713	fn scan_face(
4714	    fi: usize,
4715	    output: &Stage3Output,
4716	    ctx: &AssemblyCtx,
4717	    config: &Config,
4718	) -> Result<FaceScan, Stage3Error> {
4719	    let fd = &output.face_descriptors[fi];
4720	    let (chart, surface_override) = build_face_chart(fd, &output.stage2);
4721	    let pole_eps = pole_radius_eps(config);
4722	
4723	    let mut samples = HashMap::new();
4724	    for &ei in &fd.edge_indices {
4725	        let uv = sample_edge_uv(&ctx.topo_edges[ei], &chart, PCURVE_BASE_SAMPLES, pole_eps)
4726	            .ok_or_else(|| {
4727	                Stage3Error::AdjacencyError(format!(
4728	                    "face {fi}: edge {ei} has no stored 3D curve to project"
4729	                ))
4730	            })?;
4731	        samples.insert(ei, uv);
4732	    }
4733	
4734	    let groups = group_edges_into_wires(fd, &output.edges);
4735	    let mut loops = Vec::with_capacity(groups.len());
4736	    for group in &groups {
4737	        let items = order_loop(group, &ctx.edge_vertices)?;
4738	        loops.push(align_loop(items, &samples, &chart)?);
4739	    }
4740	
4741	    let mut wrap = WrapKind::None;
4742	    for lp in &loops {
4743	        if lp.winding_u != 0 && lp.winding_v != 0 {
4744	            return Err(Stage3Error::AdjacencyError(format!(
4745	                "face {fi}: boundary loop winds both U and V (unsupported)"
4746	            )));
4747	        }
4748	        if lp.winding_u != 0 {
4749	            if wrap == WrapKind::V {
4750	                return Err(Stage3Error::AdjacencyError(format!(
4751	                    "face {fi}: boundary loops wind both U and V (unsupported)"
4752	                )));
4753	            }
4754	            wrap = WrapKind::U;
4755	        }
4756	        if lp.winding_v != 0 {
4757	            if wrap == WrapKind::U {
4758	                return Err(Stage3Error::AdjacencyError(format!(
4759	                    "face {fi}: boundary loops wind both U and V (unsupported)"
4760	                )));
4761	            }
4762	            wrap = WrapKind::V;
4763	        }
4764	    }
4765	    let n_winding = loops
4766	        .iter()
4767	        .filter(|l| l.winding_u != 0 || l.winding_v != 0)
4768	        .count();
4769	    if n_winding > 2 {
4770	        return Err(Stage3Error::AdjacencyError(format!(
4771	            "face {fi}: {n_winding} winding boundary loops (at most 2 supported)"
4772	        )));
4773	    }
4774	    Ok(FaceScan { chart, surface_override, samples, loops, wrap })
4775	}
4776	
4777	/// The wrap-direction coordinate of a 3D point on a face's chart (U for
4778	/// U-wrapped faces, V for V-wrapped), reduced mod 2π.
4779	fn wrap_coord(chart: &SurfaceChart, kind: WrapKind, p: &[f64; 3]) -> f64 {
4780	    let (u, v, _) = chart.point_uv(p);
4781	    match kind {
4782	        WrapKind::V => v.rem_euclid(TWO_PI),
4783	        _ => u,
4784	    }
4785	}
4786	
4787	/// Congruence distance of two angles mod 2π.
4788	fn angle_dist(a: f64, b: f64) -> f64 {
4789	    let d = (a - b).rem_euclid(TWO_PI);
4790	    d.min(TWO_PI - d)
4791	}
4792	
4793	/// Choose seam anchors for every wrapped face, mutating closed-loop edges:
4794	/// each re-anchored closed edge is rebuilt to start at its anchor point and
4795	/// gains the anchor vertex at both ends. Because the anchor vertex is part
4796	/// of the shared edge, every face using that edge sees the same anchor —
4797	/// consistency across faces is forced by the sharing, not by tolerances.
4798	fn plan_seam_anchors(
4799	    output: &Stage3Output,
4800	    scans: &[Option<FaceScan>],
4801	    ctx: &mut AssemblyCtx,
4802	    config: &Config,
4803	) -> Result<HashMap<usize, WrapPlan>, Stage3Error> {
4804	    // Committed anchor parameter per closed-loop edge.
4805	    let mut anchor_t: HashMap<usize, f64> = HashMap::new();
4806	    let mut plans: HashMap<usize, WrapPlan> = HashMap::new();
4807	
4808	    // The anchor point of a winding loop, if already determined.
4809	    let anchor_point = |src: &AnchorSource, anchor_t: &HashMap<usize, f64>| -> Option<[f64; 3]> {
4810	        match src {
4811	            AnchorSource::Vertex(vid) => Some(output.vertices[*vid].point),
4812	            AnchorSource::ClosedEdge(ei) => anchor_t.get(ei).map(|&t| {
4813	                let mut f = 0.0;
4814	                let mut l = 0.0;
4815	                let curve = b_rep::Tool::curve_edge_real2(&ctx.topo_edges[*ei], &mut f, &mut l);
4816	                let p = curve.get().value(t);
4817	                [p.x(), p.y(), p.z()]
4818	            }),
4819	        }
4820	    };
4821	
4822	    // A winding loop's natural anchor source: a real vertex when the loop
4823	    // has one (lowest id for determinism), else its single closed edge.
4824	    let loop_anchor_source = |lp: &BoundaryLoop| -> Result<AnchorSource, Stage3Error> {
4825	        let mut best: Option<usize> = None;
4826	        for item in &lp.items {
4827	            for &vid in &ctx.edge_vertices[item.ei] {
4828	                if vid != usize::MAX && best.is_none_or(|b| vid < b) {
4829	                    best = Some(vid);
4830	                }
4831	            }
4832	        }
4833	        if let Some(vid) = best {
4834	            return Ok(AnchorSource::Vertex(vid));
4835	        }
4836	        if lp.items.len() == 1 {
4837	            return Ok(AnchorSource::ClosedEdge(lp.items[0].ei));
4838	        }
4839	        Err(Stage3Error::AdjacencyError(
4840	            "winding boundary loop has multiple edges but no vertices".into(),
4841	        ))
4842	    };
4843	
4844	    // Find the parameter on a closed edge where the wrap coordinate equals
4845	    // \`target\` (mod 2π), by bisection on the branch-continuous samples.
4846	    let solve_anchor_t = |ei: usize,
4847	                          uv: &EdgeUv,
4848	                          chart: &SurfaceChart,
4849	                          kind: WrapKind,
4850	                          target: f64|
4851	     -> Result<f64, Stage3Error> {
4852	        let coords = match kind {
4853	            WrapKind::V => &uv.vs,
4854	            _ => &uv.us,
4855	        };
4856	        // Lift target into the samples' branch range.
4857	        let (lo, hi) = (coords[0].min(*coords.last().unwrap()), coords[0].max(*coords.last().unwrap()));
4858	        let mut tgt = target;
4859	        while tgt < lo {
4860	            tgt += TWO_PI;
4861	        }
4862	        while tgt > hi {
4863	            tgt -= TWO_PI;
4864	        }
4865	        if tgt < lo - 1e-9 {
4866	            return Err(Stage3Error::AdjacencyError(format!(
4867	                "edge {ei}: seam azimuth not reachable on closed loop"
4868	            )));
4869	        }
4870	        let k = (0..coords.len() - 1)
4871	            .find(|&i| (coords[i] - tgt) * (coords[i + 1] - tgt) <= 0.0)
4872	            .ok_or_else(|| {
4873	                Stage3Error::AdjacencyError(format!(
4874	                    "edge {ei}: seam azimuth not bracketed on closed loop"
4875	                ))
4876	            })?;
4877	        let mut f = 0.0;
4878	        let mut l = 0.0;
4879	        let curve = b_rep::Tool::curve_edge_real2(&ctx.topo_edges[ei], &mut f, &mut l);
4880	        let c = curve.get();
4881	        let (mut ta, mut tb) = (uv.ts[k], uv.ts[k + 1]);
4882	        let (mut ca, _cb) = (coords[k], coords[k + 1]);
4883	        for _ in 0..60 {
4884	            let tm = 0.5 * (ta + tb);
4885	            let pt = c.value(tm);
4886	            let raw = wrap_coord(chart, kind, &[pt.x(), pt.y(), pt.z()]);
4887	            // Lift onto the local branch near ca.
4888	            let cm = raw - TWO_PI * ((raw - ca) / TWO_PI).round();
4889	            if (cm - tgt) * (ca - tgt) <= 0.0 {
4890	                tb = tm;
4891	            } else {
4892	                ta = tm;
4893	                ca = cm;
4894	            }
4895	        }
4896	        Ok(0.5 * (ta + tb))
4897	    };
4898	
4899	    // Fixpoint: plan faces that already have a determined anchor first, so
4900	    // chains of wrapped faces adopt a single consistent seam azimuth.
4901	    let wrapped: Vec<usize> = scans
4902	        .iter()
4903	        .enumerate()
4904	        .filter(|(_, s)| s.as_ref().is_some_and(|s| s.wrap != WrapKind::None))
4905	        .map(|(fi, _)| fi)
4906	        .collect();
4907	    let mut pending: Vec<usize> = wrapped.clone();
4908	    while !pending.is_empty() {
4909	        let mut planned_one = false;
4910	        // Two rounds: first faces with a determined anchor, then (if none
4911	        // progressed) force the lowest-index pending face with defaults.
4912	        for force in [false, true] {
4913	            let mut i = 0;
4914	            while i < pending.len() {
4915	                let fi = pending[i];
4916	                let scan = scans[fi].as_ref().unwrap();
4917	                let winding: Vec<&BoundaryLoop> = scan
4918	                    .loops
4919	                    .iter()
4920	                    .filter(|l| l.winding_u != 0 || l.winding_v != 0)
4921	                    .collect();
4922	                let sources: Vec<AnchorSource> = winding
4923	                    .iter()
4924	                    .map(|lp| loop_anchor_source(lp))
4925	                    .collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
4926	                let det: Vec<Option<[f64; 3]>> =
4927	                    sources.iter().map(|s| anchor_point(s, &anchor_t)).collect();
4928	                if !force && det.iter().all(|d| d.is_none()) {
4929	                    i += 1;
4930	                    continue;
4931	                }
4932	                // Seam azimuth from the first determined anchor, or commit
4933	                // the first loop's default anchor.
4934	                let seam_c = if let Some(p) = det.iter().flatten().next() {
4935	                    wrap_coord(&scan.chart, scan.wrap, p)
4936	                } else {
4937	                    match sources[0] {
4938	                        AnchorSource::ClosedEdge(ei) => {
4939	                            let uv = &scan.samples[&ei];
4940	                            anchor_t.insert(ei, uv.ts[0]);
4941	                            match scan.wrap {
4942	                                WrapKind::V => uv.vs[0].rem_euclid(TWO_PI),
4943	                                _ => uv.us[0].rem_euclid(TWO_PI),
4944	                            }
4945	                        }
4946	                        AnchorSource::Vertex(_) => unreachable!("vertex anchors are always determined"),
4947	                    }
4948	                };
4949	                for li in 0..winding.len() {
4950	                    match det[li] {
4951	                        Some(p) => {
4952	                            let c = wrap_coord(&scan.chart, scan.wrap, &p);
4953	                            if angle_dist(c, seam_c) > 0.01 {
4954	                                return Err(Stage3Error::AdjacencyError(format!(
4955	                                    "face {fi}: winding loops have incompatible seam anchors \
4956	                                     ({c:.4} vs {seam_c:.4} rad); edge splitting not implemented"
4957	                                )));
4958	                            }
4959	                        }
4960	                        None => {
4961	                            let AnchorSource::ClosedEdge(ei) = sources[li] else {
4962	                                unreachable!("vertex anchors are always determined")
4963	                            };
4964	                            let t = solve_anchor_t(ei, &scan.samples[&ei], &scan.chart, scan.wrap, seam_c)?;
4965	                            anchor_t.insert(ei, t);
4966	                        }
4967	                    }
4968	                }
4969	                plans.insert(fi, WrapPlan { anchors: sources });
4970	                pending.remove(i);
4971	                planned_one = true;
4972	                if force {
4973	                    break; // re-enter the determined-anchor round
4974	                }
4975	            }
4976	            if planned_one {
4977	                break;
4978	            }
4979	        }
4980	        if !planned_one {
4981	            break;
4982	        }
4983	    }
4984	
4985	    // Apply the committed anchors: rebuild each re-anchored closed edge so
4986	    // its range starts at the anchor and the anchor vertex is at both ends.
4987	    let builder = b_rep::Builder::new();
4988	    let mut anchor_edges: Vec<(usize, f64)> = anchor_t.into_iter().collect();
4989	    anchor_edges.sort_by_key(|&(ei, _)| ei);
4990	    for (ei, t) in anchor_edges {
4991	        let mut f = 0.0;
4992	        let mut l = 0.0;
4993	        let curve = b_rep::Tool::curve_edge_real2(&ctx.topo_edges[ei], &mut f, &mut l);
4994	        let period = l - f;
4995	        let t = if !curve.get().is_periodic() && (t - f).abs() > 1e-12 {
4996	            if !config.quiet {
4997	                eprintln!(
4998	                    "  Warning: edge {ei}: closed curve is not periodic; keeping its \
4999	                     existing start as the seam anchor"
5000	                );
5001	            }
5002	            f
5003	        } else {
5004	            t
5005	        };
5006	        let p = curve.get().value(t);
5007	        let mut mv = b_rep_builder_api::MakeVertex::new_pnt(&p);
5008	        let vtx = mv.vertex().to_owned();
5009	        builder.update_vertex_vertex_real(&vtx, config.vertex_tolerance_mm);
5010	        let make_edge = b_rep_builder_api::MakeEdge::new_handlegeomcurve_vertex2_real2(
5011	            &curve, &vtx, &vtx, t, t + period,
5012	        );
5013	        if !make_edge.is_done() {
5014	            return Err(Stage3Error::AdjacencyError(format!(
5015	                "MakeEdge failed while re-anchoring closed edge {ei}: {:?}",
5016	                make_edge.error(),
5017	            )));
5018	        }
5019	        let mut make_edge = make_edge;
```

6167	fn polygon_signed_area(poly: &[(f64, f64)]) -> f64 {
6168	    let mut a = 0.0;
6169	    for i in 0..poly.len() {
6170	        let p = poly[i];
6171	        let q = poly[(i + 1) % poly.len()];
6172	        a += p.0 * q.1 - q.0 * p.1;
6173	    }
6174	    0.5 * a
6175	}

2052	/// Select the intersection curve closest to the mesh boundary vertices.

2056	/// ProjectPointOnCurve can fail on certain IntSS curve representations.
2057	fn select_closest_curve(
2058	    int_ss: &geom_api::IntSS,
2059	    boundary_vertices: &[usize],
2060	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
2061	) -> i32 {
2062	    let nb_lines = int_ss.nb_lines();
2063	    let mut best_idx = 0_i32; // 0 = no valid curve found
2064	    let mut best_total_dist = f64::MAX;
2065	
2066	    // Compute centroid of boundary vertices
2067	    let mut cx = 0.0;
2068	    let mut cy = 0.0;
2069	    let mut cz = 0.0;
2070	    for &vi in boundary_vertices {

2073	        cy += v.y;
2074	        cz += v.z;

2076	    let n = boundary_vertices.len() as f64;

2078	    cy /= n;
2079	    cz /= n;

2081	    for line_idx in 1..=nb_lines {
2082	        let curve_handle = int_ss.line(line_idx);

2084	        let fp = c.first_parameter();
2085	        let lp = c.last_parameter();
2086	
2087	        // For curves with finite parameter ranges, check for extreme values
2088	        // that could cause overflow (e.g., hyperbolas from cone intersections
2089	        // whose Cosh overflows Standard_Real). Lines have infinite ranges but
2090	        // are safe — we'll use projection instead of parameter sampling.
2091	        let use_projection = !fp.is_finite() || !lp.is_finite()

2094	        if use_projection {

2098	                &centroid_pt, curve_handle,

2104	                    best_idx = line_idx;

2115	                let dy = p.y() - cy;
2116	                let dz = p.z() - cz;
2117	                let dist = (dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz).sqrt();
2118	                min_dist = min_dist.min(dist);

}

//=================================================================================================

void GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface::Init(const TColgp_Array2OfPnt&        Points,
                                          const Approx_ParametrizationType ParType,
                                          const Standard_Integer           DegMin,
                                          const Standard_Integer           DegMax,
                                          const GeomAbs_Shape              Continuity,
                                          const Standard_Real              Tol3D,
                                          const Standard_Boolean           thePeriodic)
{
  Standard_Integer Imin = Points.LowerRow();
  Standard_Integer Imax = Points.UpperRow();
  Standard_Integer Jmin = Points.LowerCol();
  Standard_Integer Jmax = Points.UpperCol();

  Standard_Real Tol2D = Tol3D;

  // first approximate the U isos:
  Standard_Integer add = 1;
  if (thePeriodic)
  {
    add = 2;
  }
  AppDef_MultiLine Line(Jmax - Jmin + 1);
  Standard_Integer i, j;

  for (j = Jmin; j <= Jmax; j++)
  {
    AppDef_MultiPointConstraint MP(Imax - Imin + add, 0);
    for (i = Imin; i <= Imax; i++)
    {
      MP.SetPoint(i, Points(i, j));
    }
    if (thePeriodic)
    {
      MP.SetPoint(Imax + 1, Points(1, j));
    }
    Line.SetValue(j, MP);
  }

  Standard_Integer nbit       = 2;
  Standard_Boolean UseSquares = Standard_False;
  if (Tol3D <= 1.e-3)
    UseSquares = Standard_True;

  AppDef_BSplineCompute
    TheComputer(DegMin, DegMax, Tol3D, Tol2D, nbit, Standard_True, ParType, UseSquares);

  switch (Continuity)
  {
    case GeomAbs_C0:
      TheComputer.SetContinuity(0);
      break;

    case GeomAbs_G1:
    case GeomAbs_C1:
      TheComputer.SetContinuity(1);
      break;

    case GeomAbs_G2:
    case GeomAbs_C2:
      TheComputer.SetContinuity(2);
      break;

    default:
      TheComputer.SetContinuity(3);
  }

  if (Tol3D <= 0.0)
  {
    TheComputer.Interpol(Line);
  }
  else
  {
    TheComputer.Perform(Line);
  }

  const AppParCurves_MultiBSpCurve& TheCurve = TheComputer.Value();

  Standard_Integer               VDegree = TheCurve.Degree();
  TColgp_Array1OfPnt             Poles(1, TheCurve.NbPoles());
  const TColStd_Array1OfReal&    VKnots = TheCurve.Knots();
  const TColStd_Array1OfInteger& VMults = TheCurve.Multiplicities();

  Standard_Integer nbisosu = Imax - Imin + add;
  AppDef_MultiLine Line2(nbisosu);

  for (i = 1; i <= nbisosu; i++)
  {
    TheCurve.Curve(i, Poles);
    AppDef_MultiPointConstraint MP(Poles.Upper(), 0);
    for (j = 1; j <= Poles.Upper(); j++)
    {
      MP.SetPoint(j, Poles(j));
    }
    Line2.SetValue(i, MP);
  }

  // approximate the resulting poles:

  AppDef_BSplineCompute
    TheComputer2(DegMin, DegMax, Tol3D, Tol2D, nbit, Standard_True, ParType, UseSquares);
  if (Tol3D <= 0.0)
  {
    if (thePeriodic)
    {
      TheComputer2.SetPeriodic(thePeriodic);
    }
    TheComputer2.Interpol(Line2);
  }
  else
  {
    if (thePeriodic && Line2.NbMultiPoints() > 2)
    {
      TheComputer2.SetPeriodic(thePeriodic);
      //
      TColStd_Array1OfReal aPars(1, Line2.NbMultiPoints());
      BuildParameters(Line2, ParType, aPars);
      math_Vector aTang(1, 3 * Poles.Upper());
      BuildPeriodicTangent(Line2, aPars, aTang);
      Standard_Integer ind = 1;
      TheCurve.Curve(ind, Poles);
      AppDef_MultiPointConstraint MP1(Poles.Upper(), 0);
      for (j = 1; j <= Poles.Upper(); j++)
      {
        MP1.SetPoint(j, Poles(j));
        Standard_Integer k = 3 * (j - 1);
        gp_Vec           aT(aTang(k + 1), aTang(k + 2), aTang(k + 3));
        MP1.SetTang(j, aT);
      }
      Line2.SetValue(ind, MP1);
      //
      ind = Line2.NbMultiPoints();
      TheCurve.Curve(ind, Poles);
      AppDef_MultiPointConstraint MP2(Poles.Upper(), 0);
      for (j = 1; j <= Poles.Upper(); j++)
      {
        MP2.SetPoint(j, Poles(j));
        Standard_Integer k = 3 * (j - 1);
        gp_Vec           aT(aTang(k + 1), aTang(k + 2), aTang(k + 3));
        MP2.SetTang(j, aT);
      }
      Line2.SetValue(ind, MP2);
    }
    TheComputer2.Perform(Line2);
  }

  const AppParCurves_MultiBSpCurve& TheCurve2 = TheComputer2.Value();

  Standard_Integer               UDegree = TheCurve2.Degree();
  TColgp_Array1OfPnt             Poles2(1, TheCurve2.NbPoles());
  const TColStd_Array1OfReal&    UKnots = TheCurve2.Knots();
  const TColStd_Array1OfInteger& UMults = TheCurve2.Multiplicities();

  // computing the surface
  TColgp_Array2OfPnt ThePoles(1, Poles2.Upper(), 1, Poles.Upper());
  for (j = 1; j <= Poles.Upper(); j++)
  {
    TheCurve2.Curve(j, Poles2);
    for (i = 1; i <= Poles2.Upper(); i++)
    {
      ThePoles(i, j) = Poles2(i);
    }
  }

  mySurface = new Geom_BSplineSurface(ThePoles, UKnots, VKnots, UMults, VMults, UDegree, VDegree);
  if (thePeriodic && Line2.NbMultiPoints() > 2)
  {
    mySurface->SetUPeriodic();
  }

  myIsDone = Standard_True;
}

//=================================================================================================

void GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface::Init(const TColgp_Array2OfPnt& Points,
                                          const Standard_Real       Weight1,
                                          const Standard_Real       Weight2,
```
</details>


## 🤖💭 Assistant
<!-- msg_011Cd4R6qJzN3BjqEYEQzKBg -->

> Looking at the OCCT source code, I can see the convention is confirmed: periodic along U means the row index is the first array index. The code appends a copy of the first row at the end, and with the constraint setup using `Imax - Imin + add` where add equals 2, it creates the proper number of points for the periodic dimension.
> 
> The interpolation path calls `SetUPeriodic()` unconditionally when the periodic flag is set and there are more than 2 multi-points — for a ring of ~93 points that's 94 points total, which easily satisfies this condition.
> 
> In the first pass, the algorithm approximates the U isos by creating an AppDef_MultiLine where each MultiPoint corresponds to a column. So the first fit actually runs along the J direction (columns), producing curves parameterized by V — these are the U isoparametric curves, one per row. The VKnots come from fitting across the J dimension, which makes sense since J maps to the V parameter. When periodic, the MultiPoint includes that extra wrapped point for each row to handle the wraparound.
> 
> Each MultiPoint represents a station along the fit parameter and holds multiple 3D channels that get fitted simultaneously. The fit produces one B-spline per channel (row i), and they all share the same knots and parameters along the J direction. So channel i's resulting curve passes through all the points in row i across the columns — that's the V-direction curve at a fixed U station.
> 
> These rows correspond to U-isos in OCCT terminology — curves at constant U parameter. The comment about approximating U-isos confirms this: rows are constant-U, columns are constant-V. The first fit adds an extra channel that duplicates the first row, so it produces Imax-Imin+2 iso curves total (rows 1 through Imax plus a wrapped copy of row 1), and these become the MultiPoints fed into the second fit.
> 
> The second fit runs along the U direction with periodic boundary conditions, treating the first and last MultiPoints as identical to close the loop. OCCT handles the duplication internally, so the input array shouldn't duplicate the closing row — the library adds that itself. The chord-length parametrization along U then comes from fitting over the pole positions of these iso curves.
> 
> For the interpolation path when Tol3D ≤ 0, I need to verify that AppDef_BSplineCompute honors SetPeriodic during the Interpol call, since the tangent-building branch only runs during Perform. A test will show empirically whether periodic interpolation produces C2 continuity across the closure.
> 
> Regarding knot multiplicities at the seam: if the periodic surface has multiplicity 1 there, C2 continuity follows by construction. I should also confirm that SetUKnots on a periodic surface handles affine scaling of knots correctly—it should, since the basis functions scale exactly with affine transformations.
> 
> Now I need to look at the existing probe binary mentioned in the plan to understand how to set up my test.

The chord-length parametrization naturally aligns with the arc-fraction seeding since both are proportional to cumulative distance, so the u_seed calculation using fractions of the knot range matches the existing convention.

```
//! Probe: conventions of GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface::Interpolate with
//! thePeriodic=true, for the §2.7 closed-band periodic pipeline
//! (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN "Path to the non-compromised design").
//!
//! Questions answered:
//!  1. Periodic along which array index? (expected: U = first index, rows,
//!     closing row omitted — OCCT appends the wrap copy itself)
//!  2. Is the result IsUPeriodic, and is closure C2?
//!  3. Does the surface interpolate the input points?
//!  4. Does SetUKnots rescale to [0, 2π] exactly (geometry unchanged)?
//!  5. Does ShapeAnalysis_Surface return principal U in [0, 2π) afterwards?
//!  6. Does UIso(0) pass through the first input row (the seam gauge)?
//!  7. Does IntSS(plane, periodic surface) return a usable closed curve?

use opencascade_sys::{
    approx, gp, geom, geom_api, shape_analysis, t_col_std, t_colgp,
};

const TWO_PI: f64 = 2.0 * std::f64::consts::PI;

fn pt(p: [f64; 3]) -> opencascade_sys::OwnedPtr<gp::Pnt> {
    gp::Pnt::new_real3(p[0], p[1], p[2])
}

fn dist(a: &gp::Pnt, b: [f64; 3]) -> f64 {
    ((a.x() - b[0]).powi(2) + (a.y() - b[1]).powi(2) + (a.z() - b[2]).powi(2)).sqrt()
}

fn main() {
    // Synthetic closed band: elliptical rings (a=27, b=10, h=18 — the
    // elliptical_cylinder model's proportions), n_around stations WITHOUT
    // the closing duplicate, n_across height stations.
    let (n_around, n_across) = (24usize, 5usize);
    let (a, b, h) = (27.0, 10.0, 18.0);
    let ring_pt = |i: usize, j: usize| -> [f64; 3] {
        let th = TWO_PI * i as f64 / n_around as f64;
        [a * th.cos(), b * th.sin(), h * j as f64 / (n_across - 1) as f64]
    };

    // First index (rows) = around/periodic axis; second = across.
    let mut pts = t_colgp::Array2OfPnt::new_int4(1, n_around as i32, 1, n_across as i32);
    for i in 0..n_around {
        for j in 0..n_across {
            pts.set_value(i as i32 + 1, j as i32 + 1, &pt(ring_pt(i, j)));
        }
    }
    let mut fit = geom_api::PointsToBSplineSurface::new();
    fit.interpolate_array2ofpnt_parametrizationtype_bool(
        &pts,
        approx::ParametrizationType::Chordlength,
        true,
    );
    println!("Q1/Q2 fit: is_done={}", fit.is_done());
    let surface = fit
        .surface()
        .to_handle_surface()
        .downcast_to_b_spline_surface()
        .expect("downcast");
    {
        let bs = surface.get();
        let (mut u0, mut u1, mut v0, mut v1) = (0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0);
        bs.bounds(&mut u0, &mut u1, &mut v0, &mut v1);
        println!(
            "  u_periodic={} v_periodic={} deg=({},{}) poles=({},{}) bounds=({:.6},{:.6})x({:.6},{:.6})",
            bs.is_u_periodic(),
            bs.is_v_periodic(),
            bs.u_degree(),
            bs.v_degree(),
            bs.nb_u_poles(),
            bs.nb_v_poles(),
            u0, u1, v0, v1,
        );
        print!("  u_knots ({}):", bs.nb_u_knots());
        for k in 1..=bs.nb_u_knots().min(6) {
            print!(" {:.4}(m{})", bs.u_knot(k), bs.u_multiplicity(k));
        }
        println!(
            " ... {:.4}(m{})",
            bs.u_knot(bs.nb_u_knots()),
            bs.u_multiplicity(bs.nb_u_knots())
        );

        // Closure continuity: D2 at u0 vs u1 (same v).
        let vm = 0.5 * (v0 + v1);
        let eval = |u: f64| {
            let mut p = gp::Pnt::new_real3(0.0, 0.0, 0.0);
            let (mut d1u, mut d1v, mut d2u, mut d2v, mut d2uv) = (
                gp::Vec::new(),
                gp::Vec::new(),
                gp::Vec::new(),
                gp::Vec::new(),
                gp::Vec::new(),
            );
            bs.d2(u, vm, &mut p, &mut d1u, &mut d1v, &mut d2u, &mut d2v, &mut d2uv);
            (
                [p.x(), p.y(), p.z()],
                [d1u.x(), d1u.y(), d1u.z()],
                [d2u.x(), d2u.y(), d2u.z()],
            )
        };
        let (p_lo, d1_lo, d2_lo) = eval(u0);
        let (p_hi, d1_hi, d2_hi) = eval(u1);
        let dd = |x: [f64; 3], y: [f64; 3]| {
            ((x[0] - y[0]).powi(2) + (x[1] - y[1]).powi(2) + (x[2] - y[2]).powi(2)).sqrt()
        };
        println!(
            "  closure: |P(u0)-P(u1)|={:.3e} |D1U delta|={:.3e} |D2U delta|={:.3e}",
            dd(p_lo, p_hi),
            dd(d1_lo, d1_hi),
            dd(d2_lo, d2_hi),
        );
    }

    // Q3: interpolation quality at the input points.
    {
        let erased = surface.to_handle_surface();
        let mut sas = shape_analysis::Surface::new_handlegeomsurface(&erased);
        let mut max_d = 0.0_f64;
        for i in 0..n_around {
            for j in 0..n_across {
                let p = ring_pt(i, j);
                let uv = sas.value_of_uv(&pt(p), 1e-7);
                let _ = uv;
                max_d = max_d.max(sas.gap());
            }
        }
        println!("Q3 interpolation: max gap at input points = {max_d:.3e}");
    }

    // Q4: rescale U knots to [0, 2*pi]; geometry must be unchanged.
    let mut before: Vec<([f64; 3], f64, f64)> = Vec::new(); // (point, u_frac, v)
    {
        let bs = surface.get();
        let (mut u0, mut u1, mut v0, mut v1) = (0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0);
        bs.bounds(&mut u0, &mut u1, &mut v0, &mut v1);
        for k in 0..7 {
            let uf = k as f64 / 7.0;
            let v = v0 + (v1 - v0) * (k as f64 / 6.0);
            let mut p = gp::Pnt::new_real3(0.0, 0.0, 0.0);
            bs.d0(u0 + (u1 - u0) * uf, v, &mut p);
            before.push(([p.x(), p.y(), p.z()], uf, v));
        }
    }
    {
        let bs = surface.get_mut();
        let nk = bs.nb_u_knots();
        let (k_first, k_last) = (bs.u_knot(1), bs.u_knot(nk));
        let mut arr = t_col_std::Array1OfReal::new_int2(1, nk);
        for k in 1..=nk {
            let scaled = (bs.u_knot(k) - k_first) / (k_last - k_first) * TWO_PI;
            arr.set_value(k, &scaled);
        }
        bs.set_u_knots(&arr);
        let (mut u0, mut u1, mut v0, mut v1) = (0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0);
        bs.bounds(&mut u0, &mut u1, &mut v0, &mut v1);
        println!(
            "Q4 rescale: new bounds=({:.6},{:.6})x({:.6},{:.6}) (want u=(0,{:.6}))",
            u0, u1, v0, v1, TWO_PI,
        );
        let mut max_d = 0.0_f64;
        for (p, uf, v) in &before {
            let mut q = gp::Pnt::new_real3(0.0, 0.0, 0.0);
            bs.d0(u0 + (u1 - u0) * uf, *v, &mut q);
            max_d = max_d.max(dist(&q, *p));
        }
        println!("  geometry drift across rescale at 7 samples: {max_d:.3e}");
    }

    // Q5: principal U from ShapeAnalysis after the rescale.
    {
        let erased = surface.to_handle_surface();
        let mut sas = shape_analysis::Surface::new_handlegeomsurface(&erased);
        let mut u_min = f64::MAX;
        let mut u_max = f64::MIN;
        for i in 0..n_around {
            let p = ring_pt(i, 2);
            let uv = sas.value_of_uv(&pt(p), 1e-7);
            u_min = u_min.min(uv.x());
            u_max = u_max.max(uv.x());
        }
        println!("Q5 ShapeAnalysis principal U range over a ring: [{u_min:.4}, {u_max:.4}]");
    }

    // Q6: UIso(0) should be the seam gauge through the first input row.
    {
        let bs = surface.get();
        let iso = bs.u_iso_real(0.0);
        let c = iso.get();
        let mut max_d = 0.0_f64;
        for j in 0..n_across {
            let target = ring_pt(0, j);
            // Sample the iso densely and take the min distance (cheap check).
            let (f, l) = (c.first_parameter(), c.last_parameter());
            let mut best = f64::MAX;
            for s in 0..200 {
                let t = f + (l - f) * s as f64 / 199.0;
                best = best.min(dist(&c.value(t), target));
            }
            max_d = max_d.max(best);
        }
        println!("Q6 UIso(0) max distance to first input row: {max_d:.3e}");
    }

    // Q7: IntSS with the cap plane z=0.
    {
        let origin = gp::Pnt::new_real3(0.0, 0.0, 0.0);
        let normal = gp::Dir::new_real3(0.0, 0.0, 1.0);
        let plane = geom::Plane::new_pnt_dir(&origin, &normal);
        let plane_h = geom::Plane::to_handle(plane).to_handle_surface();
        let surf_h = surface.to_handle_surface();
        let int_ss = geom_api::IntSS::new_handlegeomsurface2_real(&plane_h, &surf_h, 1e-7);
        println!("Q7 IntSS(plane z=0, surface): done={} nb_lines={}",
            int_ss.is_done(), int_ss.nb_lines());
        for li in 1..=int_ss.nb_lines() {
            let curve = int_ss.line(li);
            let c = curve.get();
            let (f, l) = (c.first_parameter(), c.last_parameter());
            let p_f = c.value(f);
            let p_l = c.value(l);
            let closed = dist(&p_f, [p_l.x(), p_l.y(), p_l.z()]) < 1e-6;
            let mut max_d = 0.0_f64;
            for i in 0..n_around {
                let target = ring_pt(i, 0);
                let mut best = f64::MAX;
                for s in 0..400 {
                    let t = f + (l - f) * s as f64 / 399.0;
                    best = best.min(dist(&c.value(t), target));
                }
                max_d = max_d.max(best);
            }
            println!(
                "  line {li}: params [{f:.4},{l:.4}] closed={closed} periodic={} \
                 max dist to bottom ring pts={max_d:.3e}",
                c.is_periodic(),
            );
        }
    }
}

```
</details>

I could reduce the seam multiplicity via knot removal to achieve C2, but that would alter the geometry slightly—not worth the trade-off since C1 closure is sufficient for this application.

220	    pub fn distance_seeded(&self, p: &[f64; 3], seed_frac: (f64, f64)) -> f64 {
221	        let (mut u0, mut u1, mut v0, mut v1) = (0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0);

    let sampler = UvSampler::new(&region, &uv);

    // Fit modes, in order of preference: a cut band tries the periodic fit
    // around the band first, then falls back to the clamped fit that gets
    // seam-split into two faces sharing the surface.
    let modes: Vec<Option<bool>> = if cut {
        vec![Some(cut_around_axis(&region, &uv)?), None]
    } else {
        vec![None]
    };
    let mut last_err = String::new();
    for around in modes {

            // boundary can be much coarser than the region it bounds (flat
            // margins tessellate coarsely — an 8-vertex side on a 946-vertex
            // region).
            let target = ((region.verts.len() as f64).sqrt() * density).round() as usize;
            let target = target.clamp(8, cap);
            let u_stations = axis_stations(&u_side_a, &u_side_b, target, &maps.u_profile);
            let v_stations = axis_stations(&v_side_a, &v_side_b, target, &maps.v_profile);
            match fit_grid(

                    } else {

                    };
                }
                Err(e) => {

                        eprintln!(
                            "  Stage 2.7: {}x{}{} grid attempt failed: {e}",
                            u_stations.len(),
                            v_stations.len(),
                            if around.is_some() { " periodic" } else { "" },
                        );
                    }
                    // A collapsed parameterization won't improve with density.
                    if e.contains("parameterization covers") {
                        return Err(e);
                    }
                    last_err = e;
                }
            }
        }
    }
    Err(last_err)
}

/// Which unit-square axis runs around a seam-cut band (from cut copy to cut
/// copy): the cut vertices sit at that axis's parameter extremes.
fn cut_around_axis(region: &Region, uv: &[(f64, f64)]) -> Result<bool, String> {
    let cut_uv = region
        .loops
        .iter()
        .flatten()
        .find(|&&(_, out)| out == CUT_OUTSIDE)
        .map(|&(li, _)| uv[li])
        .ok_or("seam-cut region has no cut boundary")?;
    Ok(cut_uv.0.min(1.0 - cut_uv.0) < cut_uv.1.min(1.0 - cut_uv.1))
}

/// Split the fitted hypothesis of a seam-cut closed band into two halves
/// along the around-the-band axis. Both halves share the SAME fitted
/// surface object; the caller tags them with a common
/// `shared_surface_group` so downstream code can recognize the two edges
/// between them as reconstruction artifacts (they have no counterpart in a
/// reference model, which would use one periodic face with a seam). Each
/// half spans only part of the surface's parameter range, so its two cut
/// edges are geometrically distinct and no periodic-seam machinery is
/// needed.
fn split_cut_hypothesis(
    hyp: BSplineHypothesis,
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    region: &Region,
    uv: &[(f64, f64)],
) -> Result<Vec<BSplineHypothesis>, String> {
    // The around axis runs from cut copy to cut copy: cut vertices sit at
    // that axis's parameter extremes.
    let cut_uv = region
        .loops
        .iter()
        .flatten()
        .find(|&&(_, out)| out == CUT_OUTSIDE)
        .map(|&(li, _)| uv[li])
        .ok_or("seam-cut region has no cut boundary")?;
    let around_u = cut_uv.0.min(1.0 - cut_uv.0) < cut_uv.1.min(1.0 - cut_uv.1);
```

**After:**
```
/// FALLBACK for closed bands the periodic fit cannot validate: split the
/// clamped fitted hypothesis of a seam-cut band into two halves along the
/// around-the-band axis. Both halves share the SAME fitted surface object.
/// Each half spans only part of the surface's parameter range, so its two
/// cut edges are geometrically distinct and no periodic-seam machinery is
/// needed — but those two edges are reconstruction artifacts a reference
/// model (one periodic face with a real seam) does not have, so the
/// stage 3.1 edge compare will flag them.
fn split_cut_hypothesis(
    hyp: BSplineHypothesis,
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    region: &Region,
    uv: &[(f64, f64)],
) -> Result<Vec<BSplineHypothesis>, String> {
    // The around axis runs from cut copy to cut copy.
    let around_u = cut_around_axis(region, uv)?;

fn fit_grid(

    // Orient the grid so the fitted surface's natural normal (d1u × d1v)
    // points outward, matching the mesh winding normals. Fixing the
    // convention at fit time (one row flip) beats recovering it later with
    // per-face heuristics.
    let idx = |i: usize, j: usize| i * n_v + j;
    let (ci, cj) = (n_u / 2, n_v / 2);
    let du = sub3(grid[idx(ci + 1, cj)], grid[idx(ci - 1, cj)]);
    let dv = sub3(grid[idx(ci, cj + 1)], grid[idx(ci, cj - 1)]);
    let grid_n = cross3(du, dv);
    let center = grid[idx(ci, cj)];
    let mesh_n = region_normal_near(mesh, region, center);
    let flip = dot3(grid_n, mesh_n) < 0.0;

    let mut pts = t_colgp::Array2OfPnt::new_int4(1, n_u as i32, 1, n_v as i32);
    for i in 0..n_u {
        for j in 0..n_v {
            let jj = if flip { n_v - 1 - j } else { j };
            let p = grid[idx(i, jj)];
            let pnt = gp::Pnt::new_real3(p[0], p[1], p[2]);
            pts.set_value(i as i32 + 1, j as i32 + 1, &pnt);
        }
    }
```

**After:**
```
    // Orient the grid so the fitted surface's natural normal (d1u × d1v)
    // points outward, matching the mesh winding normals. Fixing the
    // convention at fit time (one row flip) beats recovering it later with
    // per-face heuristics.
    let idx = |i: usize, j: usize| i * n_v + j;
    let (ci, cj) = (n_u / 2, n_v / 2);
    let du = sub3(grid[idx(ci + 1, cj)], grid[idx(ci - 1, cj)]);
    let dv = sub3(grid[idx(ci, cj + 1)], grid[idx(ci, cj - 1)]);
    let grid_n = cross3(du, dv);
    let center = grid[idx(ci, cj)];
    let mesh_n = region_normal_near(mesh, region, center);
    let flip = dot3(grid_n, mesh_n) < 0.0;

    // Array layout: the fitted surface's U is the array's FIRST index. A
    // periodic fit needs the around-the-band axis there (transpose when the
    // around axis is v), with its closing station — the second cut copy,
    // whose column duplicates the first — omitted: OCCT's periodic
    // Interpolate appends the wrap-around copy of the first row itself
    // (probed, see DEVELOPMENT_PLAN §2.7). Orientation: transposing negates
    // d1u×d1v, so the outward flip reverses the SECOND index and accounts
    // for the transpose.
    let (n_a, n_b, transpose) = match around {
        Some(false) => (n_v - 1, n_u, true),
        Some(true) => (n_u - 1, n_v, false),
        None => (n_u, n_v, false),
    };
    let flip_b = flip != transpose;
    let mut pts = t_colgp::Array2OfPnt::new_int4(1, n_a as i32, 1, n_b as i32);
    for a in 0..n_a {
        for b in 0..n_b {
            let bb = if flip_b { n_b - 1 - b } else { b };
            let (i, j) = if transpose { (bb, a) } else { (a, bb) };
            let p = grid[idx(i, j)];
            let pnt = gp::Pnt::new_real3(p[0], p[1], p[2]);
            pts.set_value(a as i32 + 1, b as i32 + 1, &pnt);
        }
    }

    // Interpolation first: the grid is sampled from an exact-on-surface
    // mesh, so a C2 cubic surface through every node is the most faithful
    // reconstruction (OCCT's least-squares approximator reports IsDone
    // while overshooting its Tol3D by millimeters near strongly curved
    // patch boundaries — measured on the saddle model). The approximator
    // remains as a fallback for data interpolation can't handle — except on
    // the periodic path, which is interpolation-only (the approximator
    // binding carries no periodic flag).
    let approx_tols = [
        (config.surface_tolerance_mm / 8.0).max(1e-4),
        (config.surface_tolerance_mm / 2.0).max(1e-4),
    ];
    let methods: &[Option<f64>] = if around.is_some() {
        &[None]
    } else {
        &[None, Some(approx_tols[0]), Some(approx_tols[1])]
    };
    let mut last_err = String::new();
    for &method in methods {
        let surface = std::panic::catch_unwind(std::panic::AssertUnwindSafe(|| {
            let fit = match method {
                None => {
                    let mut fit = geom_api::PointsToBSplineSurface::new();
                    fit.interpolate_array2ofpnt_parametrizationtype_bool(
                        &pts,
                        approx::ParametrizationType::Chordlength,
                        around.is_some(),
                    );
                    fit
                }
                Some(tol3d) => {
                    geom_api::PointsToBSplineSurface::new_array2ofpnt_parametrizationtype_int2_shape_real(
                        &pts,
                        approx::ParametrizationType::Chordlength,
                        3,
                        8,
                        geom_abs::Shape::C2,
                        tol3d,
                    )
                }
            };
            if fit.is_done() {
                fit.surface().to_handle_surface().downcast_to_b_spline_surface()
            } else {
                None
            }
        }));
        let mut surface = match surface {
            Ok(Some(s)) => s,
            Ok(None) => {
                last_err = "GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface did not converge".to_string();
                continue;
            }
            Err(_) => {
                last_err = "GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface raised an exception".to_string();
                continue;
            }
        };
        if around.is_some() {
            // Rescale the periodic (around) axis's knots to exactly [0, 2π]
            // — an exact affine reparameterization (probed: geometry drift
            // ~1e-15). Stage 3.4's wrapped-face machinery (branch unwrap,
            // winding, pcurve shifts, seam placement) hard-codes that
            // period.
            let bs = surface.get_mut();
            if !bs.is_u_periodic() {
                last_err = "periodic interpolation returned a non-periodic surface".to_string();
                continue;
            }
            let nk = bs.nb_u_knots();
            let (k0, k1) = (bs.u_knot(1), bs.u_knot(nk));
            let mut knots = t_col_std::Array1OfReal::new_int2(1, nk);
            for k in 1..=nk {
                let scaled = (bs.u_knot(k) - k0) / (k1 - k0) * std::f64::consts::TAU;
                knots.set_value(k, &scaled);
            }
            bs.set_u_knots(&knots);
        }
        match validate_fit(
            mesh, region, sampler.uv, maps, around, flip_b, surface, n_u, n_v, &grid, config,
        ) {
            Ok(hyp) => return Ok(hyp),
            Err(e) => last_err = e,
        }
    }
    Err(last_err)
}

## 🤖🔧 Assistant

```
/// Validate a fitted surface: every region vertex and face centroid must
/// lie within surface tolerance. Each projection is seeded from the
/// vertex's own Floater UV (`uv`, indexed like `region.verts`), the one
/// reliable way to find the true nearest point on a many-knot surface.
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
fn validate_fit(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    region: &Region,
    uv: &[(f64, f64)],
    maps: &AxisMaps,
    flip: bool,
    surface: OwnedPtr<geom::HandleGeomBSplineSurface>,
    n_u: usize,
    n_v: usize,
    grid: &[[f64; 3]],
    config: &Config,
) -> Result<BSplineHypothesis, String> {
    // Hypothesis vertices are unique GLOBAL mesh vertices (region.verts may
    // repeat globals for seam-cut clones).
    let mut hyp_verts = region.verts.clone();
    hyp_verts.sort_unstable();
    hyp_verts.dedup();
    let mut hyp = BSplineHypothesis::new(surface, region.faces.clone(), hyp_verts);
    let tol = config.surface_tolerance_mm;
    // The fit's parameter is ~linear in arc fraction (the grid stations
    // equalize arc), and its v axis is reversed when the grid was flipped
    // for outward orientation.
    let seed_of = |li: usize| -> (f64, f64) {
        let (u, v) = uv[li];
        let gu = AxisMaps::arc_fraction(&maps.u_profile, u);
        let gv = AxisMaps::arc_fraction(&maps.v_profile, v);
        (gu, if flip { 1.0 - gv } else { gv })
    };
```

**After:**
```
/// Validate a fitted surface: every region vertex and face centroid must
/// lie within surface tolerance. Each projection is seeded from the
/// vertex's own Floater UV (`uv`, indexed like `region.verts`), the one
/// reliable way to find the true nearest point on a many-knot surface.
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
fn validate_fit(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    region: &Region,
    uv: &[(f64, f64)],
    maps: &AxisMaps,
    around: Option<bool>,
    flip_b: bool,
    surface: OwnedPtr<geom::HandleGeomBSplineSurface>,
    n_u: usize,
    n_v: usize,
    grid: &[[f64; 3]],
    config: &Config,
) -> Result<BSplineHypothesis, String> {
    // Hypothesis vertices are unique GLOBAL mesh vertices (region.verts may
    // repeat globals for seam-cut clones).
    let mut hyp_verts = region.verts.clone();
    hyp_verts.sort_unstable();
    hyp_verts.dedup();
    let mut hyp = BSplineHypothesis::new(surface, region.faces.clone(), hyp_verts);
    hyp.u_periodic = around.is_some();
    let tol = config.surface_tolerance_mm;
    // The fit's parameter is ~linear in arc fraction (the grid stations
    // equalize arc). Map the Floater axes onto the fitted surface's axes:
    // transposed when the band's around axis is v (the surface's U is
    // always the array's first index), with the second axis reversed when
    // the grid was flipped for outward orientation.
    let seed_of = |li: usize| -> (f64, f64) {
        let (u, v) = uv[li];
        let gu = AxisMaps::arc_fraction(&maps.u_profile, u);
        let gv = AxisMaps::arc_fraction(&maps.v_profile, v);
        let (ga, gb) = if around == Some(false) { (gv, gu) } else { (gu, gv) };
        (ga, if flip_b { 1.0 - gb } else { gb })
    };
```

/// True when the two faces are B-spline halves of one fitted surface (the
/// deliberate seam-split of a closed freeform band): edges between them —
/// and the corner vertices those edges end at — are reconstruction
/// artifacts with no counterpart in a reference model, which represents
/// the band as a single periodic face with a seam.
fn is_seam_cut_pair(
    fi0: usize,
    fi1: usize,
    face_descriptors: &[FaceDescriptor],
    stage2: &Stage2Output,
) -> bool {
    let s0 = &stage2.selected_surfaces[face_descriptors[fi0].selected_surface_idx];
    let s1 = &stage2.selected_surfaces[face_descriptors[fi1].selected_surface_idx];
    match (s0, s1) {
        (SelectedSurface::BSpline(a), SelectedSurface::BSpline(b)) => {
            a != b
                && stage2.bspline_hypotheses[*a].shared_surface_group.is_some()
                && stage2.bspline_hypotheses[*a].shared_surface_group
                    == stage2.bspline_hypotheses[*b].shared_surface_group
        }
        _ => false,
    }
}

/// Compare stage 3.1 adjacency graph against reference STEP shape.
///
/// For each ReconEdge, samples mesh boundary vertices along the edge and checks
/// that each is within vertex_tolerance of a STEP edge.
/// For each BRepVertex, checks that it is within vertex_tolerance of a STEP vertex.
/// Seam-cut edges (and the vertices they end at) are exempt: they are
/// deliberate reconstruction artifacts flagged at fit time, not features.
fn compare_adjacency_to_step(
    edges: &[ReconEdge],
    vertices: &[BRepVertex],

**After:**

struct BSplineChartData {
    surf: OwnedPtr<geom::HandleGeomSurface>,
    sas: RefCell<OwnedPtr<shape_analysis::Surface>>,
    seed: std::cell::Cell<Option<(f64, f64)>>,
    /// Parameter bounds of the surface.
    bounds: (f64, f64, f64, f64),
    /// True for the periodic fit of a closed freeform band: U-periodic with
    /// knots rescaled to [0, 2π], so the chart behaves like the analytic
    /// periodic charts (principal U values, wrapped-face machinery).
    u_periodic: bool,
    /// Parameter-space anchor inside this face's own region. The two halves
    /// of a seam-split closed band (the fallback reconstruction) share a
    /// surface whose parameter-extreme boundary curves are the SAME 3D
    /// curve, so a projection landing on it is ambiguous between the two
    /// extremes; the branch nearer the face's own interior wins.
    home: std::cell::Cell<(f64, f64)>,
}

    /// A result on a parameter boundary whose opposite boundary carries the
    /// same 3D curve (a seam-split closed band) is ambiguous: keep the
    /// branch nearer this face's `home` anchor. Coincidence is verified in
    /// 3D, so ordinary (non-closed) patch boundaries are never touched.
    fn resolve_boundary_branch(&self, u: f64, v: f64) -> (f64, f64) {
        let (u0, u1, v0, v1) = self.bounds;
        let home = self.home.get();
        let mut out = (u, v);
        let eps_u = 1e-3 * (u1 - u0);
        let eps_v = 1e-3 * (v1 - v0);
        if (v - v0).abs() < eps_v || (v1 - v).abs() < eps_v {
            let here = if (v - v0).abs() < eps_v { v0 } else { v1 };
            let there = if here == v0 { v1 } else { v0 };
            let a = self.point_at(u, here);
            let b = self.point_at(u, there);
            let d2 = (a[0] - b[0]).powi(2) + (a[1] - b[1]).powi(2) + (a[2] - b[2]).powi(2);
            if d2 < 1e-12 && (there - home.1).abs() < (here - home.1).abs() {
                out.1 = there;
            }
        }
        if (u - u0).abs() < eps_u || (u1 - u).abs() < eps_u {
            let here = if (u - u0).abs() < eps_u { u0 } else { u1 };
            let there = if here == u0 { u1 } else { u0 };
            let a = self.point_at(here, out.1);

                out.0 = there;
            }
        }
        out
    }

        }

In our case, the IntSS returned a span of exactly 2π, so the deviation check passes and we skip the circle reconstruction entirely—which is good because fitting a circle to an elliptical junction would be incorrect. For a general freeform band,  a partial-arc result on a closed junction would trigger circle reconstruction and likely fail the chain deviation check, pushing it toward the B-spline repair arm, but that's a deferred concern since the closed-chain periodic interpolation isn't needed for this model.

    // Ground-truth validation: the mesh boundary chain lies on the true
    // junction within chordal tolerance, so the trimmed curve must pass
    // close to the chain. When it doesn't — the classic complementary-arc
    // trap on a periodic junction, a degenerate IntSS result with an odd
    // parameterization, or a straight-line stand-in for a curved junction —
    // rebuild the curve as the circle through the chain and re-trim with a
    // chain-supported arc. The chain is the one representation available
    // for every junction, so this guard is uniform across surface pairs.
    let final_curve = repair_edge_curve_from_boundary(
        final_curve, edge, vertices, mesh, config, fi0, fi1,
    );

    edge.curve_3d = Some(final_curve);
    Ok(())
}
```

**After:**
```
    // Ground-truth validation: the mesh boundary chain lies on the true
    // junction within chordal tolerance, so the trimmed curve must pass
    // close to the chain. When it doesn't — the classic complementary-arc
    // trap on a periodic junction, a degenerate IntSS result with an odd
    // parameterization, or a straight-line stand-in for a curved junction —
    // rebuild the curve as the circle through the chain and re-trim with a
    // chain-supported arc. The chain is the one representation available
    // for every junction, so this guard is uniform across surface pairs.
    let final_curve = repair_edge_curve_from_boundary(
        final_curve, edge, vertices, mesh, config, fi0, fi1,
    );

    // Closed-loop edges must carry a PERIODIC curve: stage 3.4's seam
    // planning re-anchors them with MakeEdge over [t, t + period] at an
    // arbitrary seam azimuth. Analytic closed junctions (circles) already
    // are; IntSS junctions against a fitted B-spline surface arrive as
    // closed-but-non-periodic trimmed curves and are converted (exact —
    // probed at ~1e-14).
    let final_curve = if edge.vertex_indices[0] == usize::MAX
        && edge.vertex_indices[1] == usize::MAX
    {
        periodicize_closed_curve(final_curve, config, fi0, fi1)
    } else {
        final_curve
    };

    edge.curve_3d = Some(final_curve);
    Ok(())
}

/// Convert a closed-loop edge's closed-but-non-periodic curve into a
/// periodic Geom_BSplineCurve (same geometry, same parameter range). Keeps
/// the input curve when it is already periodic, is not actually closed, or
/// the conversion fails.
fn periodicize_closed_curve(
    curve: OwnedPtr<geom::HandleGeomCurve>,
    config: &Config,
    fi0: usize,
    fi1: usize,
) -> OwnedPtr<geom::HandleGeomCurve> {
    {
        let c = curve.get();
        if c.is_periodic() {
            return curve;
        }
        let p = c.value(c.first_parameter());
        let q = c.value(c.last_parameter());
        let gap = ((p.x() - q.x()).powi(2) + (p.y() - q.y()).powi(2) + (p.z() - q.z()).powi(2))
            .sqrt();
        if gap > config.vertex_tolerance_mm {
            return curve; // not closed; nothing to periodicize
        }
    }
    let converted = std::panic::catch_unwind(std::panic::AssertUnwindSafe(|| {
        let mut bsc = geom_convert::curve_to_b_spline_curve_handlegeomcurve_parameterisationtype(
            &curve,
            convert::ParameterisationType::Tgtthetaover2,
        );
        bsc.get_mut().set_periodic();
        bsc.to_handle_curve()
    }));
    match converted {
        Ok(periodic) => {
            if config.verbose {
                eprintln!(
                    "    Edge (f{fi0},f{fi1}): closed junction curve made periodic \
                     for seam re-anchoring"
                );
            }
            periodic
        }
        Err(_) => {
            if !config.quiet {
                eprintln!(
                    "  Warning: edge (f{fi0},f{fi1}): could not make the closed junction \
                     curve periodic; seam re-anchoring may keep its original start"
                );
            }
            curve
        }
    }
}
```

> The periodic path approach is working well—I've fitted a B-spline, the comparison checks are passing with minimal edge deviation, and the seam detection and face assembly are complete with the shell closed and solid built. However, there are still two issues to resolve, starting with an edge problem that got cut off. The asymmetry is puzzling — the bottom junction works fine but the top one fails, even though the geometry is symmetric. I suspect the issue is that IntSS is returning a closed curve parameterized over [0,1] that covers the full ellipse, but my partial-arc detection only checks if the span equals 2π. For a closed non-circular intersection with span 1.0, it incorrectly triggers the "reconstruct as circle" logic. The real fix is to detect whether the curve is actually closed (endpoints match) rather than relying on the parameter span — a closed curve is already complete regardless of how it's parameterized.
> 
> On the bottom cap, the pcurve deviation is slightly elevated at 1.22e-2 mm, likely from the Tgtthetaover2 parameterization shift, though it's still within acceptable bounds.  The issue is that after converting the curve to a B-spline with this new parameterization, the parameter spacing becomes non-uniform, and when interpolating the pcurve across 33 to 257 samples, the deviation plateaus around that value. The deviation itself measures the difference between evaluating the chart at the pcurve parameter versus the 3D curve at the same parameter, and on a planar chart this should theoretically be exact for an ellipse.
> 
> The bottom junction curve's parameter range of [-6.2832, 0] came from the seam re-anchoring process — SetPeriodic solved for the seam azimuth and rebuilt the edge with that anchor point, which happened to be -6.2832. The 1.22e-2 mm pcurve deviation on the plane isn't necessarily a problem since BRepCheck passed and the face was created successfully; it just needs to be covered by the edge tolerance and SameParameter settings, and the STEP file comparison validated it.
> 
> The real issue is the 12% volume error from the top cap being reconstructed as a circle instead of an ellipse. I need to fix the partial-arc detection logic by checking the endpoint gap — only attempt circle reconstruction if the endpoints are actually far enough apart to indicate a genuinely partial arc.
> 
> Fixing partial-arc detection... Checking whether the top IntSS curve actually closed or came back as a partial arc — if it's genuinely partial with endpoints apart, the circle reconstruction logic should trigger, but I need to verify what the span value tells us about whether the endpoints are actually separated. For closed chains that form loops, I'll use GeomAPI_Interpolate with the periodic flag set to true, passing the chain vertices and tolerance to generate a periodic B-spline curve as a candidate repair. I need to check how the mesh boundary vertices are stored—whether the first and last points are duplicated or distinct—and then call the interpolation with periodic mode enabled to fit a smooth closed curve through them.
> 
> My plan is to fix the partial-arc detection to check for endpoint gaps rather than span, add the periodic-chain interpolation candidate to the repair function for closed chains (which also resolves the other deferred TODO), and then rerun the tests.

static void BuildParameters(const Standard_Boolean         PeriodicFlag,
                            const TColgp_Array1OfPnt&      PointsArray,
                            Handle(TColStd_HArray1OfReal)& ParametersPtr)
{
  Standard_Integer ii, index;
  Standard_Real    distance;
  Standard_Integer num_parameters = PointsArray.Length();
  if (PeriodicFlag)
  {
    num_parameters += 1;
  }
  ParametersPtr = new TColStd_HArray1OfReal(1, num_parameters);
  ParametersPtr->SetValue(1, 0.0e0);
  index = 2;
  for (ii = PointsArray.Lower(); ii < PointsArray.Upper(); ii++)
  {
    distance = PointsArray.Value(ii).Distance(PointsArray.Value(ii + 1));
    ParametersPtr->SetValue(index, ParametersPtr->Value(ii) + distance);
    index += 1;
  }
  if (PeriodicFlag)
  {
    distance =
      PointsArray.Value(PointsArray.Upper()).Distance(PointsArray.Value(PointsArray.Lower()));
    ParametersPtr->SetValue(index, ParametersPtr->Value(ii) + distance);
  }
}

> So for periodic interpolation in GeomAPI_Interpolate, the points are provided without a closing duplicate—the wrap-around chord gets calculated from the last point back to the first. The CheckPoints validation ensures no two consecutive points are identical within tolerance, which would catch most degenerate cases, though the wrap chord distance itself could still become zero and create parameter issues.
> 
> Now I'm implementing the fix for partial-arc detection by checking the endpoint gap. I'm calculating the distance between the first and last parameter values on the curve, and if that gap exceeds ten times the vertex tolerance, it indicates a genuinely partial arc that needs reconstruction using the three-point circle method. Otherwise, if the endpoints are close enough, the curve is already a complete loop regardless of its parameter span. The debug message will continue to reference the span for clarity, and the sampled points still use span fractions which works fine for the partial case.
> 
> For the second fix, I'm adding periodic chain-interpolation as a candidate in the repair function. When dealing with closed chains that have no endpoint vertices and at least four points, I'm using periodic C2 interpolation through the loop rather than open approximation. The implementation checks if the chain has a duplicated closing vertex and removes it before passing the points to the interpolation routine, since the periodic flag handles the closure automatically.
> 
> Building the interpolation curve... I'm settling on a tolerance value derived from the config—taking the vertex tolerance in millimeters, scaling it down by a factor of 100, and ensuring it doesn't drop below 1e-9, which gives me a reasonable threshold for detecting coincident chain points. The interpolation curve should come back as a B-spline handle that I can convert to the appropriate curve type. Checking the chain comparison logic for closed loops where the first and last vertices might be identical, and verifying the bindings for the point array and interpolation methods.

    // For closed-loop edges, IntSS may return partial arcs (e.g., semicircles)
    // when the intersection circle passes through the sphere's UV poles (the
    // intersection plane contains the sphere axis). In UV space, such a circle
    // splits into two disconnected arcs, which IntSS returns separately.
    // Detect this and reconstruct the full circle from sampled curve points.
    let full_circle_curve: Option<OwnedPtr<geom::HandleGeomCurve>> =
        if edge.vertex_indices[0] == usize::MAX && edge.vertex_indices[1] == usize::MAX {
            let c = initial_curve_handle.get();
            let span = c.last_parameter() - c.first_parameter();
            if (span - 2.0 * std::f64::consts::PI).abs() > 0.1 {
                // Partial arc — reconstruct full circle from 3 sampled points
                let fp = c.first_parameter();
                let p0 = c.value(fp);
                let p1 = c.value(fp + span * 0.25);
                let p2 = c.value(fp + span * 0.75);
```

**After:**
```
    // For closed-loop edges, IntSS may return partial arcs (e.g., semicircles)
    // when the intersection circle passes through the sphere's UV poles (the
    // intersection plane contains the sphere axis). In UV space, such a circle
    // splits into two disconnected arcs, which IntSS returns separately.
    // Detect this and reconstruct the full circle from sampled curve points.
    // Partiality is decided by the endpoint gap, NOT the parameter span: a
    // closed junction with a B-spline surface comes back as a closed curve
    // over an arbitrary range (observed [0,1]), and treating it as a
    // partial arc would replace the true junction with a bogus circle.
    let full_circle_curve: Option<OwnedPtr<geom::HandleGeomCurve>> =
        if edge.vertex_indices[0] == usize::MAX && edge.vertex_indices[1] == usize::MAX {
            let c = initial_curve_handle.get();
            let span = c.last_parameter() - c.first_parameter();
            let p_first = c.value(c.first_parameter());
            let p_last = c.value(c.last_parameter());
            let endpoint_gap = ((p_first.x() - p_last.x()).powi(2)
                + (p_first.y() - p_last.y()).powi(2)
                + (p_first.z() - p_last.z()).powi(2))
            .sqrt();
            if endpoint_gap > 10.0 * config.vertex_tolerance_mm {
                // Partial arc — reconstruct full circle from 3 sampled points
                let fp = c.first_parameter();
                let p0 = c.value(fp);
                let p1 = c.value(fp + span * 0.25);
                let p2 = c.value(fp + span * 0.75);
```
</details>

    // Candidate 2: a B-spline curve through the chain vertices — a
    // least-squares fit trimmed to the endpoints for open chains, a
    // periodic C2 interpolation for closed chains (a closed freeform
    // junction, e.g. a cap meeting a fitted band, that IntSS missed). The
    // chain is ground truth for any junction, so an ordered fit through it
    // handles freeform junction curves no analytic construction covers.
    if has_endpoints && chain.len() >= 4 {
        let mut pts = t_colgp::Array1OfPnt::new_int2(1, chain.len() as i32);
        for (k, &vi) in chain.iter().enumerate() {
            let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
            let p = gp::Pnt::new_real3(v.x, v.y, v.z);
            pts.set_value(k as i32 + 1, &p);
        }
        let fit_tol = (config.surface_tolerance_mm / 10.0).max(config.vertex_tolerance_mm * 10.0);
        let fit = std::panic::catch_unwind(std::panic::AssertUnwindSafe(|| {
            geom_api::PointsToBSpline::new_array1ofpnt_int2_shape_real(
                &pts,
                3,
                8,
                geom_abs::Shape::C2,
                fit_tol,
            )
        }));
        if let Ok(fit) = fit {
            if fit.is_done() {
                let full = fit.curve().to_handle_curve();
                if let Some(c) = trim_to_endpoints(&full) {
                    let d = boundary_chain_deviation(&c, chain, mesh);
                    if d < best_dev {
                        best_dev = d;
                        best = Some((c, "B-spline"));
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    } else if !has_endpoints && chain.len() >= 4 {
        // Closed chain: GeomAPI_Interpolate with the periodic flag (the
        // closing point is NOT repeated — the wrap chord is implied).
        let mut loop_chain: &[usize] = chain;
        if loop_chain.first() == loop_chain.last() {
            loop_chain = &loop_chain[..loop_chain.len() - 1];
        }
        let mut pts_h = t_colgp::HArray1OfPnt::new_int2(1, loop_chain.len() as i32);
        for (k, &vi) in loop_chain.iter().enumerate() {
            let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
            let p = gp::Pnt::new_real3(v.x, v.y, v.z);
            pts_h.change_array1().set_value(k as i32 + 1, &p);
        }
        let pts_handle = t_colgp::HArray1OfPnt::to_handle(pts_h);
        let fit = std::panic::catch_unwind(std::panic::AssertUnwindSafe(|| {
            let mut interp = geom_api::Interpolate::new_handletcolgpharray1ofpnt_bool_real(
                &pts_handle,
                true,
                (config.vertex_tolerance_mm * 0.01).max(1e-9),
            );
            interp.perform();
            if interp.is_done() {
                Some(interp.curve().to_handle_curve())
            } else {
                None
            }
        }));
        if let Ok(Some(c)) = fit {
            let d = boundary_chain_deviation(&c, chain, mesh);
            if d < best_dev {
                best_dev = d;
                best = Some((c, "periodic B-spline"));
            }
        }
    }

/// elliptical_cylinder: a closed freeform wall (annulus topology) is
/// seam-cut, fitted once, and split into two B-spline faces sharing the
/// fitted surface — tagged as one shared-surface group so stage 3 treats
/// the edges between them as reconstruction artifacts.
#[test]
fn nurbs_elliptical_cylinder_stage27_selection() {
    let stl = format!("{}/tests/nurbs/elliptical_cylinder.stl", manifest_dir());
    let step = format!("{}/tests/nurbs/elliptical_cylinder.step", manifest_dir());
    let config = config_for_compare_stage27(&stl, &step);
    let output = run_stage2(&config);
    let (planar, bspline) = planar_bspline_selection_counts(&output);
    assert_eq!(bspline, 2, "closed wall should split into two B-spline faces");
    assert_eq!(planar, 2, "top and bottom caps");
    let groups: Vec<Option<usize>> = output
        .bspline_hypotheses
        .iter()
        .map(|h| h.shared_surface_group)
        .collect();
    assert_eq!(groups[0], groups[1], "both halves carry one group tag");
    assert!(groups[0].is_some(), "the halves must be tagged as seam-split");
}
```

**After:**
```
/// elliptical_cylinder: a closed freeform wall (annulus topology) is
/// seam-cut for flattening, then fitted as ONE U-periodic B-spline surface
/// (knots rescaled to [0, 2π]) that stage 3 reconstructs as a single
/// wrapped face with a real seam edge — the same form CAD kernels write.
#[test]
fn nurbs_elliptical_cylinder_stage27_selection() {
    let stl = format!("{}/tests/nurbs/elliptical_cylinder.stl", manifest_dir());
    let step = format!("{}/tests/nurbs/elliptical_cylinder.step", manifest_dir());
    let config = config_for_compare_stage27(&stl, &step);
    let output = run_stage2(&config);
    let (planar, bspline) = planar_bspline_selection_counts(&output);
    assert_eq!(bspline, 1, "closed wall is ONE periodic B-spline face");
    assert_eq!(planar, 2, "top and bottom caps");
    let hyp = &output.bspline_hypotheses[0];
    assert!(hyp.u_periodic, "closed-band fit must be U-periodic");
}
```

<summary>Edit DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md</summary>

**Before:**

4. **Parameterize** the region onto the unit square: outer boundary loop pinned by cumulative chord length, 4 corners chosen where the adjacent *outside* selection changes (arc-length-quartile fallback when the boundary offers ≠ 4 transitions), interior and hole-loop vertices solved with Floater mean-value weights by Gauss–Seidel (convergence enforced — an unconverged map voids Floater's bijectivity guarantee). Quads are split along the v0–v2 diagonal for weighting and sampling. **Closed bands** (a second boundary loop of extent ≥ half the largest — a small hole floats freely, a comparable second loop means the region wraps, e.g. the wall of a closed prism) are first **seam-cut** into a disk along a shortest vertex path between the two loops (`cut_region_seam`): the path follows FACE perimeter edges only (a path along a quad's split diagonal would tear that quad across the cut), its vertices are duplicated, right-side triangle fans switch to the clones, and the boundary re-chains into one loop whose two cut copies carry a `CUT_OUTSIDE` id — corner detection then lands the 4 corners exactly at the cut endpoints. The clones keep their originals' positions, so the sampled parameter-extreme grid columns carry identical data and the fitted (clamped, non-periodic) surface closes exactly: its two boundary iso-curves share a pole column and coincide.
```

**After:**
```markdown
4. **Parameterize** the region onto the unit square: outer boundary loop pinned by cumulative chord length, 4 corners chosen where the adjacent *outside* selection changes (arc-length-quartile fallback when the boundary offers ≠ 4 transitions), interior and hole-loop vertices solved with Floater mean-value weights by Gauss–Seidel (convergence enforced — an unconverged map voids Floater's bijectivity guarantee). Quads are split along the v0–v2 diagonal for weighting and sampling. **Closed bands** (a second boundary loop of extent ≥ half the largest — a small hole floats freely, a comparable second loop means the region wraps, e.g. the wall of a closed prism) are first **seam-cut** into a disk along a shortest vertex path between the two loops (`cut_region_seam`): the path follows FACE perimeter edges only (a path along a quad's split diagonal would tear that quad across the cut), its vertices are duplicated, right-side triangle fans switch to the clones, and the boundary re-chains into one loop whose two cut copies carry a `CUT_OUTSIDE` id — corner detection then lands the 4 corners exactly at the cut endpoints. The clones keep their originals' positions, so the two parameter-extreme grid columns of the around-the-band axis carry identical data (the periodic fit drops the closing one; the clamped fallback fit keeps both and closes exactly through the shared column).
```
</details>

6. **Fit**: `GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface::Interpolate` (chord-length, C2 cubic) first — the grid is sampled from an exact-on-surface mesh, and OCCT's least-squares approximator reports `IsDone` while overshooting its `Tol3D` by millimeters near strongly curved patch boundaries. The approximator (tol = surface/8, then surface/2) remains as fallback, and grid density retries at caps 121/181/241. **Closed bands fit periodically**: `Interpolate(…, thePeriodic=true)` with the around-the-band axis as the array's FIRST index (transposed when that axis is v; the outward-orientation flip then reverses the second index and accounts for the transpose's normal negation) and the closing station omitted — OCCT appends the wrap-around copy of the first row itself and calls `SetUPeriodic()`. The periodic path is interpolation-only (the approximator binding carries no periodic flag). The resulting closure is C1, not C2 (position and D1 match across the wrap to ~1e-14; D2 jumps — measured), which nothing downstream requires. The fitted around-axis knots are then rescaled to exactly [0, 2π] via `SetUKnots` — an exact affine reparameterization (~1e-15 drift, measured) — because stage 3.4's wrapped-face machinery hard-codes that period. The U=0 iso is then exactly the cut path (chord-length parameters start at the first supplied row), a free seam gauge.
```
